L_{z^{\prime}}=\mathsf{LE}\left(S^{z^{\prime}}_{m}\right)_{m=0,\ldots,\tau_{L_{0}}\left(S^{z^{\prime}}\right)}

3)

The random walk
S^{z}
runs, and it hit
L_{0}
at
0
before hitting
L_{z^{\prime}}.

Introduce the event
\EuScript{N}(z,z^{\prime})=\left\{\tau_{0}(S^{z})<\tau_{L_{z^{\prime}}}(S^{z})\right\},
which is the event where
S^{z}
hits
L_{0}
at zero before it touches
L_{z^{\prime}}
anywhere. The aforementioned first three steps show that

(E)

\textstyle\EuScript{C}(z)\cap\EuScript{H}(z^{\prime})\subset\EuScript{N}(z,z^{\prime}).

In what follows, we will consider the planes
\mathrm{H}_{p}=\left\{4\cdot 9^{2pk_{0}}\right\}\times\mathds{Z}^{2},
and for
z,z^{\prime}\in\mathds{Z}^{3},
the hitting times
\sigma_{p}(z,z^{\prime})=\tau_{\mathrm{H}_{p}+z^{\prime}}(S^{z}).
As such, we will make a “change of scales” and consider from now onwards indices of the form
4\cdot 9^{2pk_{0}}.
Assume then that
z^{\prime}\in\mathrm{C}_{4\cdot 9^{2pk_{0}},0}^{\prime}
and
z\in\mathrm{C}_{4\cdot 9^{2pk_{0}},0}(z^{\prime}).
Denote
a_{p}=-4\cdot 9^{2pk_{0}}+4\cdot 9^{2(p-1)k_{0}},
so that
\mathrm{H}_{p-1}+z^{\prime}=\{a_{p}\}\times\mathds{Z}^{2}.
Consider the events

\begin{split}\EuScript{E}_{p}&\textstyle=\left\{S^{0}\text{ ever reaches }-9^{pk_{0}}\right\},\\
\EuScript{S}_{p}(z^{\prime})&\textstyle=\bigcup\limits_{h=a_{p}-2\cdot 9^{(p-1)k_{0}}}^{a_{p}}\left\{h\text{ is a splitting level for }\left(S^{z^{\prime}}_{m^{\prime}}\right)_{m^{\prime}\in\mathds{Z}_{+}}\right\}.\end{split}

By (
2.17
) and (
5.11
) we know there exists a constant
c>0
and an index
p_{4}\in\mathds{N}
such that for all
p\geq p_{4},

\mathds{P}{}\left(\EuScript{E}_{p}\right)=e^{-\lambda 9^{pk_{0}}}
and
\mathds{P}{}\left(\EuScript{S}_{p}(z^{\prime})\right)\geq 1-e^{-c9^{pk_{0}}}.
Hence,

(F)

\textstyle\mathds{P}{}\left(\EuScript{N}(z,z^{\prime})\right)\leq\mathds{P}{}\left(\EuScript{N}(z,z^{\prime})\cap\EuScript{S}_{p}(z^{\prime})\cap\EuScript{E}_{p}^{\complement}\right)+e^{-c9^{pk_{0}}}+e^{-\lambda 9^{2pk_{0}}}.

We introduce the following “good event”
\EuScript{G}_{p}(z^{\prime})=\EuScript{S}_{p}(z^{\prime})\cap\EuScript{E}_{p}^{\complement};
on this event
S^{0}
never goes left of the plane
\{-9^{pk_{0}}\}\times\mathds{Z}^{2}
and
S^{z^{\prime}}
has a splitting level in the stated range. On the good event
\EuScript{G}_{p},
the random walk
S^{z^{\prime}}
must have crossed the plane
\mathrm{H}_{p-1}+z^{\prime}
before hitting the first branch. In particular,
\sigma_{p-1}(z^{\prime},z^{\prime})<\tau_{L_{0}}\left(S^{z^{\prime}}\right)<\infty\quad\mathrm{on}\quad\EuScript{G}_{p}(z^{\prime}).
Also, on the good event
\EuScript{G}_{p},
there is a splitting plane for
\left(S^{z^{\prime}}_{m^{\prime}}\right)_{m^{\prime}\in\mathds{Z}_{+}}
; introduce
h_{p}(z^{\prime})
and
m_{p}(z^{\prime})\leq\sigma_{p-1}(z^{\prime},z^{\prime})
to be the last splitting level (amongst the levels
h=a_{p}-2\cdot 9^{(p-1)k_{0}},\ldots,a_{p}
) and the only index
m^{\prime}
such that
\mathrm{pr}_{0}\left(S^{z^{\prime}}_{m^{\prime}}\right)=h_{p}(z^{\prime}).
By (
5.10
), on the good event,

\textstyle L_{z^{\prime}}=\mathsf{LE}\left(S^{z^{\prime}}_{m^{\prime}}\right)_{m^{\prime}=0,\ldots,\tau_{L_{0}\left(S^{z^{\prime}}\right)}}=\mathsf{LE}\left(S^{z^{\prime}}_{m^{\prime}}\right)_{m^{\prime}=0,\ldots,m_{p}(z^{\prime})}\vee\mathsf{LE}\left(S^{z^{\prime}}_{m^{\prime}}\right)_{m^{\prime}=m_{p}(z^{\prime}),\ldots,\tau_{L_{0}\left(S^{z^{\prime}}\right)}}.

Consider now the “partial branch at
z^{\prime}
(until its hitting time of
\mathrm{H}_{p-1}+z^{\prime}
)” which is

\textstyle L^{z^{\prime}}_{p-1}\mathop{=}\limits^{\mathrm{def.}}\mathsf{LE}\left(S^{z^{\prime}}_{m^{\prime}}\right)_{m^{\prime}=0,\ldots,\sigma_{p-1}(z^{\prime},z^{\prime})}.

On
\EuScript{G}_{p}(z^{\prime}),

\textstyle L^{z^{\prime}}_{p-1}=\mathsf{LE}\left(S^{z^{\prime}}_{m^{\prime}}\right)_{m^{\prime}=0,\ldots,\sigma_{p-1}(z^{\prime},z^{\prime})}=\mathsf{LE}\left(S^{z^{\prime}}_{m^{\prime}}\right)_{m^{\prime}=0,\ldots,m_{p}(z^{\prime})}\vee\mathsf{LE}\left(S^{z^{\prime}}_{m^{\prime}}\right)_{m^{\prime}=m_{p}(z^{\prime}),\ldots,\sigma_{p-1}(z^{\prime},z^{\prime})}.

Define
\alpha_{p-1}(z,z^{\prime})=\sum\limits_{m=0,\ldots,\sigma_{p-1}(z,z^{\prime})}\sum\limits_{z^{\prime\prime}\in L^{z^{\prime}}_{p-1}}\mathds{1}_{\{S_{m}^{z}=z^{\prime\prime}\}},
which is the number of intersections between the partial branch at
z^{\prime},
and the section
(S_{m}^{z})_{m=0,\ldots,\sigma_{p-1}(z,z^{\prime})}.
Similarly, define
\beta_{p-1}(z,z^{\prime})
to be the number of intersections between
L_{z^{\prime}}
and the same section of
S^{z},
that is,
\beta_{p-1}(z,z^{\prime})=\sum\limits_{m=0,\ldots,\sigma_{p-1}(z,z^{\prime})}\sum\limits_{z^{\prime\prime}\in L_{z^{\prime}}}\mathds{1}_{\{S_{m}^{z}=z^{\prime\prime}\}}.
We have the following limit
\lim\limits_{p\to\infty}\max\limits_{\left\lVert z-z^{\prime}\right\rVert\leq\delta_{0}}\mathds{P}{}\left(\alpha_{p-1}(z,z^{\prime})=0\right)=0,
where the maximum runs over all pairs
(z,z^{\prime})\in\mathrm{C}_{4\cdot 9^{2pk_{0}},0}\times\mathrm{C}_{4\cdot 9^{2pk_{0}},0}^{\prime}.
This last limit is a corollary of proposition (
5.13
): the translation invariance of the probability kernel shows at once that the maximum inside the limit does not depend on the pair
(z,z^{\prime})
above but solely on the distance separating them.

Lemma ( 5.15.1
)

\displaystyle\lim_{p\to\infty}\max_{\left\lVert z-z^{\prime}\right\rVert\leq\delta_{0}}\mathds{P}{}\left(\EuScript{G}_{p}(z^{\prime})\cap\{\beta_{p-1}(z,z^{\prime})=0\}\right)=0,
where the maximum runs over all pairs
(z,z^{\prime})\in\mathrm{C}_{4\cdot 9^{2pk_{0}},0}\times\mathrm{C}_{4\cdot 9^{2pk_{0}},0}^{\prime}.

Proof of lemma.

Observe that

\textstyle\mathds{P}{}\left(\EuScript{G}_{p}(z^{\prime})\cap\{\beta_{p-1}(z,z^{\prime})=0\}\right)\leq\mathds{P}{}\left(\EuScript{G}_{p}(z^{\prime})\cap\{\alpha_{p-1}(z,z^{\prime})>0,\beta_{p-1}(z,z^{\prime})=0\}\right)+\mathds{P}{}\left(\alpha_{p-1}(z,z^{\prime})=0\right).

The second of these two terms tends uniformly to zero for the set of stated pairs
(z,z^{\prime}),
as was shown before the lemma. On the good event
\EuScript{G}_{p}
we can apply the two decompositions above for
L_{z^{\prime}}
and
L^{z^{\prime}}_{p-1},
giving

\textstyle\EuScript{G}_{p}\cap\{\alpha_{p-1}(z,z^{\prime})>0,\beta_{p-1}(z,z^{\prime})=0\}\subset\left\{S^{z}\text{ cross }S^{z^{\prime}}\text{ inside }\mathrm{J}_{p-1}\right\},

where
\mathrm{J}_{p-1}=\left\{a_{p}-2\cdot 9^{(p-1)k_{0}},\ldots,a_{p}\right\}\times\mathds{Z}^{2}.
By (
5.9
), we have that there exists a universal constant
c>0
and an index
p_{3}
such that for
p\geq p_{3},

\mathds{P}{}\left(S^{z}\text{ cross }S^{z^{\prime}}\text{ inside }\mathrm{J}_{p-1}\right)\leq c3^{-pk_{0}},
this bound being uniform for
z^{\prime}\in\mathrm{C}_{4\cdot 9^{2pk_{0},0}}^{\prime}
and
z\in\mathrm{C}_{4\cdot 9^{2pk_{0}},0}(z^{\prime}).

∎

We continue now with the proof of the theorem. Recall we consider
z^{\prime}\in\mathrm{C}_{4\cdot 9^{2pk_{0}},0}^{\prime}
and
z\in\mathrm{C}_{4\cdot 9^{2pk_{0}},0}(z^{\prime}).
Introduce the following random time
\theta_{p-1}(z,z^{\prime})=\min\left(\sigma_{p-1}(z,z^{\prime}),\tau_{L_{z^{\prime}}}(S^{z})\right)
which is the hitting time of
(\mathrm{H}_{p-1}+z^{\prime})\cup L_{z^{\prime}}
by
S^{z}.
Then,
\theta_{p-1}(z,z^{\prime})
is a stopping time for the filtration

\textstyle\left(\mathscr{X}_{k}^{z^{\prime}}(z)=\sigma\left(S^{z}_{m}\ \middle|\ 0\leq m\leq k\right)\vee\mathscr{F}_{\infty}\left(\{0,z^{\prime}\}\right)\right)_{k\in\mathds{Z}_{+}},

where
\mathscr{F}_{\infty}(\mathrm{T})=\sigma(S^{t}_{m}\mid t\in\mathrm{T},m\in\mathds{Z}_{+}).
Observe that
\EuScript{G}_{p}(z^{\prime})\in\mathscr{F}_{\infty}(\{0,z^{\prime}\}).
We apply the strong Markov property to the process
\left(S^{z}_{k},\mathscr{X}_{k}^{z^{\prime}}(z)\right)_{k\in\mathds{Z}_{+}},

(G)

\textstyle\mathds{E}{}\left(\mathds{1}_{\EuScript{N}(z,z^{\prime})\cap\EuScript{G}_{p}(z^{\prime})}\right)=\mathds{E}{}\left(\mathds{E}{}\left(\mathds{1}_{\EuScript{N}(z,z^{\prime})}\mathds{1}_{\EuScript{G}_{p}(z^{\prime})}\ \middle|\ \mathscr{X}_{\theta_{p-1}(z,z^{\prime})}^{z^{\prime}}(z)\right)\right)=\mathds{E}{}\left(\mathds{1}_{\EuScript{G}_{p}(z^{\prime})}\mathds{P}{}\left(\EuScript{N}(z,z^{\prime})\mid S_{\theta_{p-1}(z,z^{\prime})}^{z}\right)\right).

The definition of the stopping time
\theta_{p-1}(z,z^{\prime})
shows that
\mathds{P}{}\left(\EuScript{N}(z,z^{\prime})\middle|S_{\theta_{p-1}(z,z^{\prime})}^{z}=z^{\prime\prime}\right)=0
for all
z^{\prime\prime}\in L_{z^{\prime}}
lying to the left or on the plane
\mathrm{H}_{p-1}+z^{\prime}.
Therefore, we have

\textstyle\mathds{P}{}\left(\EuScript{N}(z,z^{\prime})\middle|S_{\theta_{p-1}(z,z^{\prime})}^{z}\right)=\mathds{P}{}\left(\EuScript{N}(z,z^{\prime})\middle|S_{\theta_{p-1}(z,z^{\prime})}^{z}\right)\mathds{1}_{\left\{S_{\theta_{p-1}(z,z^{\prime})}^{z}\notin L_{z^{\prime}}\right\}}.

Clearly
\EuScript{N}(z,z^{\prime})\subset\{\tau_{0}(S^{z})<\infty\},
also,
\theta_{p-1}(z,z^{\prime})=\sigma_{p-1}(z,z^{\prime}),
on the event
\left\{S_{\theta_{p-1}(z,z^{\prime})}^{z}\notin L_{z^{\prime}}\right\};
this implies
S_{\theta_{p-1}(z,z^{\prime})}^{z}\in\mathrm{H}_{p-1}+z^{\prime}
on this event. Whence,

(H)

\textstyle\mathds{P}{}\left(\EuScript{N}(z,z^{\prime})\middle|S_{\theta_{p-1}(z,z^{\prime})}^{z}\right)\leq\left(\sup\limits_{z^{\prime\prime}\in\mathrm{H}_{p-1}+z^{\prime}}\mathds{P}{}\left(\tau_{0}(S^{z})<\infty\mid S_{0}^{z}=z^{\prime\prime}\right)\right)\mathds{1}_{\left\{S_{\theta_{p-1}(z,z^{\prime})}^{z}\notin L_{z^{\prime}}\right\}}.

We know from the bounds of the Green’s function that

(I)

\textstyle\sup\limits_{z^{\prime\prime}\in\mathrm{H}_{p-1}+z^{\prime}}\mathds{P}{}\left(\tau_{0}(S^{z})<\infty\mid S^{z}_{0}=z^{\prime\prime}\right)\leq c_{1}|a_{p}|^{-1}\leq c9^{-2pk_{0}},

where
c>0
is a universal constant.

Use the bound of (
I
) in (
H
), and then apply this to (
G
). Thus, we have shown up to this point in the argument the existence of a universal constant
c>0
such that

\textstyle\mathds{P}{}\left(\EuScript{N}(z,z^{\prime})\cap\EuScript{G}_{p}(z^{\prime})\right)\leq c9^{-2pk_{0}}\mathds{P}{}\left(\EuScript{G}_{p}(z^{\prime})\cap\left\{S_{\theta_{p-1}(z,z^{\prime})}^{z}\notin L_{z^{\prime}}\right\}\right).

It is also obvious that
\EuScript{G}_{p}(z^{\prime})\cap\left\{S_{\theta_{p-1}(z,z^{\prime})}^{z}\notin L_{z^{\prime}}\right\}\subset\EuScript{G}_{p}(z^{\prime})\cap\{\beta_{p-1}(z,z^{\prime})=0\}.
Hence,

(J)

\textstyle\mathds{P}{}\left(\EuScript{N}(z,z^{\prime})\cap\EuScript{G}_{p}(z^{\prime})\right)\leq c9^{-2pk_{0}}\Delta_{p},

with
\Delta_{p}=\max\limits_{\left\lVert z-z^{\prime}\right\rVert\leq\delta_{0}}\mathds{P}{}\left(\EuScript{G}_{p}(z^{\prime})\cap\{\beta_{p-1}(z,z^{\prime})=0\}\right)\to 0
as
p\to\infty,
the pairs
(z,z^{\prime})\in\mathrm{C}_{4\cdot 9^{2pk_{0}},0}\times\mathrm{C}_{4\cdot 9^{2pk_{0}},0}^{\prime}.

We are in conditions to conclude the proof of the theorem. By the foregoing,

\displaystyle\mathds{P}{}\left(\EuScript{C}(z)\cap\EuScript{H}(z^{\prime})\right)

\displaystyle\leq\mathds{P}{}\left(\EuScript{N}(z,z^{\prime})\right),

use (
E
)

\displaystyle\leq\mathds{P}{}\left(\EuScript{N}(z,z^{\prime})\cap\EuScript{G}_{p}(z^{\prime})\right)+e^{-c^{\prime}9^{pk_{0}}}+e^{-\lambda 9^{-2pk_{0}}},

use (
F
)

\displaystyle\leq c9^{-2pk_{0}}\Delta_{p}+e^{-c^{\prime}9^{pk_{0}}}+e^{-\lambda 9^{-2pk_{0}}},

use (
J
)

with the two constants
c,c^{\prime}>0
being universal. We consider now
z^{\prime}\in\mathrm{C}_{4\cdot 9^{2pk_{0}},0}^{\prime}
and sum over
z\in\mathrm{C}_{4\cdot 9^{2pk_{0}},0}(z^{\prime}),
this latter set has cardinality bounded above by
c^{\prime\prime}\delta_{0}^{2},
with
c^{\prime\prime}>0
another universal constant. This shows, for every
z^{\prime}\in\mathrm{C}_{4\cdot 9^{2pk_{0}},0}^{\prime},

\textstyle\sum\limits_{z\in\mathrm{C}_{4\cdot 9^{2pk_{0}},0}(z^{\prime})}\mathds{P}{}\left(\EuScript{C}(z)\cap\EuScript{H}(z^{\prime})\right)\leq c^{\prime\prime}\delta_{0}^{2}\left(c9^{-2pk_{0}}\Delta_{p}+e^{-c^{\prime}9^{pk_{0}}}+e^{-\lambda 9^{-2pk_{0}}}\right),

this bound being uniform for
z^{\prime}.
We know also that
\mathrm{card}\left(\mathrm{C}_{4\cdot 9^{2pk_{0}},0}^{\prime}\right)\leq\mathrm{card}\left(\mathrm{C}_{4\cdot 9^{2pk_{0}},0}\right)\leq c^{\prime\prime\prime}\cdot 9^{2pk_{0}},
for yet another universal constant
c^{\prime\prime\prime}>0.
Whence, if we sum over
z^{\prime}\in\mathrm{C}_{4\cdot 9^{2pk_{0}},0}^{\prime},
we reach a bound of the form

\textstyle\sum\limits_{z^{\prime}\in\mathrm{C}_{4\cdot 9^{2pk_{0}},0}^{\prime}}\sum\limits_{z\in\mathrm{C}_{4\cdot 9^{2pk_{0}},0}(z^{\prime})}\mathds{P}{}\left(\EuScript{C}(z)\cap\EuScript{H}(z^{\prime})\right)\leq c\left(\Delta_{p}+9^{2pk_{0}}\left(e^{-c^{\prime}9^{pk_{0}}}+e^{-\lambda 9^{2pk_{0}}}\right)\right),

for a constant
c>0,
not depending on
p.
The right hand side of this inequality tends to zero, and this completes the proof of Theorem (
5.15
) by (D).
∎

5.4
One endedness for
\Gamma_{d}(\lambda),
with
d\geq 3

We finally deal with the case of “high dimensions.” Here, the proof is considerably simpler than
d=2.
We will make use of several general results of electrical networks, see
[
LP16
]
or
[
MD20
, Appendix C]
.

Theorem ( 5.16
)

Let
d\geq 3.
Then, for almost every realisation of the uniform spanning forest in
\Gamma_{d}(\lambda),
all the components in this realisation have one end.

Proof.

For every
p\in\mathds{N},
consider the sets
\mathrm{A}_{p},

\mathrm{B}_{p},

\mathrm{C}_{p},
defined in proposition (
5.6
). Using the notation of this proposition,
p^{\frac{1}{4}}
will be larger than
B
starting at some index
p^{*}.
As such, we may divide
\mathrm{C}_{p}=\mathrm{C}_{p,0}\cup\mathrm{C}_{p,1},
with
\mathrm{C}_{p,0}=\{-p\}\times\mathrm{B}\left(0;p^{\frac{3}{4}}\right)
and
\mathrm{C}_{p,1}=\mathrm{C}_{p}\setminus\mathrm{C}_{p,0}.

( 5.16.1
)

For every
p\geq p^{*},

\sum\limits_{z\in\mathrm{A}_{p}\cup\mathrm{B}_{p}}G(z,0)\leq ce^{-c^{\prime}p}
and
\sum\limits_{z\in\mathrm{C}_{p,1}}G(z,0)\leq ce^{-c^{\prime}p^{\frac{1}{2}}},
where
c,c^{\prime}
are positive constants.

Assume
\mathfrak{F}
is the random spanning forest of
\Gamma_{d}(\lambda)
as constructed by Wilson’s algorithm rooted at infinity. To be more precise, assume that on some probability space
(\Omega,\mathscr{F},\mathds{P})
there is a family of independent network random walks of
\Gamma_{d}(\lambda),
say
(S^{z})_{z\in\mathds{Z}^{d+1}}
with
S^{z}
started at
z,
and then construct
\mathfrak{F}
using some predefined order starting at zero of
\mathds{Z}^{d+1}
following Wilson’s algorithm. (For instance, start at zero and then search the
\left\lVert\cdot\right\rVert_{1}
-spheres by first increasing the radii one unit at a time and then lexicographically ordering each sphere.) We know then that
\mathfrak{F}
is a random spanning forest following law
\mathsf{USF}
of
\Gamma_{d}(\lambda);
this signifies that
\mathds{P}{}\left(\mathfrak{F}\in\mathscr{E}\right)=\mathsf{USF}(\mathscr{E}),
for all events
\mathscr{E}
in the probability space of
\mathsf{USF}
spanning forests of
\Gamma_{d}(\lambda).

Because of the order chosen to construct
\mathfrak{F},

L_{0}=\mathsf{LE}(S^{0}_{m})_{m\in\mathds{Z}_{+}}
is (a.s.) an infinite path, we will call it “first branch of the random forest
\mathfrak{F}.
” Define the following set in
\mathds{Z}^{d+1}
(consider
z\in\mathds{Z}^{d+1}
) and events in
\mathscr{F}:

\textstyle\begin{split}\mathrm{E}_{p}&=\mathrm{A}_{p}\cup\mathrm{B}_{p}\cup\mathrm{C}_{p,1},\\
\EuScript{E}(z)&=\{z\text{ is connected to }0\text{ in }\mathfrak{F}\setminus L_{0}\}\\
\EuScript{E}_{p}&\textstyle=\bigcup\limits_{z\in\mathrm{E}_{p}}\EuScript{E}(z)=\{\text{some vertex in }\mathrm{E}_{p}\text{ is connected to }0\text{ in }\mathfrak{F}\setminus L_{0}\}.\end{split}

Then,
\mathds{P}{}\left(\EuScript{E}_{p}\right)\leq\sum\limits_{z\in\mathrm{E}_{p}}\mathds{P}{}\left(\EuScript{E}(z)\right).
To calculate
\mathds{P}{}\left(\EuScript{E}(z)\right)
we may apply corollary (
5.5.1
) and assume that
\mathfrak{F}
was constructed using the ordering
(0,z,\ldots)
of
\mathds{Z}^{d+1}.
With this ordering, the event
\EuScript{E}(z)
is the event where
S^{z}
hits
L_{0}
for the first time at vertex
0,
whence
\mathds{P}{}\left(\EuScript{E}_{p}\right)\leq\sum\limits_{z\in\mathrm{E}_{p}}\mathds{P}{}\left(\tau_{0}(S^{z})<\infty\right)\leq\sum\limits_{z\in\mathrm{E}_{p}}G(z,0)<ce^{-c^{\prime}p^{\frac{1}{2}}},
where the last inequality follows from (
5.16.1
). By virtue of Borel-Cantelli lemma, we reach the existence of a random index
P:\Omega\to\mathds{Z}_{+}
(measurable relative to
\mathscr{F}
) such that for
\mathds{P}
-a.e.
\omega\in\Omega,

\omega\notin\bigcup\limits_{p\geq P(\omega)}\EuScript{E}_{p}.

Let
\mathfrak{U}
denote the (random connected) component of
0
in the forest
\mathfrak{F}\setminus L_{0}.
Denote by
\mathfrak{U}_{p}
the graph induced by
\mathfrak{U}
in the cylinder
\mathrm{V}_{p}=[-p,p]\times\mathrm{B}\left(0;p\right)
of
\mathds{Z}\times\mathds{Z}^{d}.

Lemma ( 5.16.2
)

If
\mathds{P}{}\left(\mathfrak{U}\text{ is finite}\right)=1,
then
\mathds{P}{}\left(\text{all the components of }\mathfrak{F}\text{ have one end}\right)=1.

Proof of lemma.

For every
z\in\mathds{Z}^{d+1},
denote by
\EuScript{T}(z)
the event where the component of
z
in
\mathfrak{F}
is not one ended. Then,

\textstyle\EuScript{T}\mathop{=}\limits^{\text{def.}}\{\text{there exists a component of }\mathfrak{F}\text{ that is not one ended}\}=\bigcup\limits_{z\in\mathds{Z}^{d+1}}\EuScript{T}(z).

We want to prove
\mathds{P}{}\left(\EuScript{T}\right)=0.
By translation invariance,
\mathds{P}{}\left(\EuScript{T}(z)\right)
is independent of
z,
and therefore, it suffices to prove that
\EuScript{T}(0)
is a null event.

Now, since
0
is a vertex of
L_{0}\subset\mathfrak{F}
and this is an infinite branch (a.s.), the relation that the component of
0
in
\mathfrak{F}
is not one ended signifies that
\mathfrak{U}
does not possess a ray, and this is equivalent to
\mathfrak{U}
being finite since it is a locally finite tree. We established that
\EuScript{T}(0)^{\complement}=\{\mathfrak{U}\text{ is finite}\}.
The conclusion of the lemma is now clear.
∎

We know that for
p\geq P,
no edge in
\mathfrak{U}
is adjacent to
\mathrm{E}_{p},
and the only way
\mathfrak{U}
can have infinitely many edges is by using edges adjacent to
\mathrm{C}_{p,0}.
Let
\mathfrak{K}_{p-1}
be the (random) set of edges of
\mathrm{V}_{p}=[-p,p]\times\mathrm{B}\left(0;p\right)
that are adjacent to
\mathfrak{U}_{p-1}
and
\mathrm{C}_{p,0}.
We need to study the size of the sets
\mathfrak{K}_{p},
the following lemma will be needed.

Lemma ( 5.16.3
)

Suppose that
\mathfrak{A}
is a random spanning subgraph of
\mathrm{G}
defined on some probability space
(\Omega,\mathscr{F},\mathds{P}).
Suppose that
\mathfrak{A}
satisfies the following: if
v
and
v^{\prime}
are two vertices that are end-points of edges of
\mathfrak{A},
then
v
and
v^{\prime}
are in the same connected-component relative to
\mathfrak{A}.
(Think of
\mathfrak{A}
as a connected subgraph of
\mathrm{G}
plus all the vertices of
\mathrm{G}.
) Denote by
(\mathrm{V}_{n})
an exhaustion of
\mathrm{G}
such that
\overline{\mathrm{V}}_{n}\subset\mathrm{V}_{n+1}
for every
n\in\mathds{N}.
Let
\mathscr{F}_{n}
denote the
\sigma
-algebra generated by the events
\{e\in\mathfrak{A}\}
as
e
runs through the edges of the graph induced on
\mathrm{V}_{n}.
Define
\mathfrak{A}_{n}
to be the spanning subgraph of
\mathrm{G}
whose edge set consists of the edges of
\mathfrak{A}
with both end-points belonging to
\mathrm{V}_{n}
(with
\mathfrak{A}_{0}=\varnothing
). Consider the events (
n\in\mathds{N}
)
\EuScript{G}_{n}=\{\mathfrak{A}_{n}\neq\mathfrak{A}_{n-1}\},
so that,
\EuScript{G}_{n}
is the event where there was “growth by edges” of
\mathfrak{A}
in
\mathrm{V}_{n}.
Set
Y_{n}=\mathds{P}{}\left(\EuScript{G}_{n}\mid\mathscr{F}_{n-1}\right)
(
n\in\mathds{N}
). Then
(\EuScript{G}_{n})_{n\in\mathds{N}}
is a decreasing sequence, if
\EuScript{G}_{\infty}=\bigcap\limits_{n\in\mathds{N}}\EuScript{G}_{n},
then
\EuScript{G}_{\infty}
is the event where
\mathfrak{A}
has infinite edges; furthermore, on
\EuScript{G}_{\infty},

Y_{n}\to 1.

Proof of lemma.

It is clear that
\EuScript{G}_{n}^{\complement}\subset\EuScript{G}_{n+1}^{\complement},
hence
(\EuScript{G}_{n})
is decreasing. Next,
1\geq\mathds{P}{}\left(\bigcup\limits_{n\in\mathds{N}}\EuScript{G}_{n}^{\complement}\cap\EuScript{G}_{n-1}\right)=\sum\limits_{n\in\mathds{N}}\mathds{E}{}\left(\mathds{E}{}\left(\mathds{1}_{\EuScript{G}_{n}^{\complement}}\mathds{1}_{\EuScript{G}_{n-1}}\middle|\mathscr{F}_{n-1}\right)\right).
It is clear that
\EuScript{G}_{n-1}\in\mathscr{F}_{n-1}
and so,
1\geq\mathds{E}{}\left(\sum\limits_{n\in\mathds{N}}\mathds{1}_{\EuScript{G}_{n-1}}(1-Y_{n})\right).
Therefore, the positive random variable
\sum\limits_{n\in\mathds{N}}\mathds{1}_{\EuScript{G}_{n-1}}(1-Y_{n})
is finite for
\mathds{P}
-a.e. realisation
\omega\in\Omega.
Thus,
\sum\limits_{n\in\mathds{N}}(1-Y_{n})
is finite
\mathds{P}
-a.s. on the event
\EuScript{G}_{\infty},
which immediately implies the conclusion to be reached, namely, that
Y_{n}\to 1
on
\EuScript{G}_{\infty}.

∎

Let us return to the proof of Theorem (
5.16
). We apply the previous lemma to the random graph
\mathfrak{U},
and the exhaustion
\mathrm{V}_{p}=[-p,p]\times\mathrm{B}\left(0;p\right).
Then, the events constructed in the lemma are
\EuScript{G}_{p}=\{\mathfrak{U}_{p}\neq\mathfrak{U}_{p-1}\}
and the
\sigma
-algebras are
\mathscr{F}_{p}=\sigma(\{e\in\mathfrak{U}_{p}\};e\text{ is an edge of the graph induced on }\mathrm{V}_{p}\text{ by }\mathds{Z}^{d+1}).
By construction,
\mathfrak{K}_{p-1}
is
\mathscr{F}_{p-1}
-measurable. Set
Y_{p}=\mathds{P}{}\left(\EuScript{G}_{p}\mid\mathscr{F}_{p-1}\right),
so that
\mathds{P}
-a.s.
Y_{p}\to 1
on the event where
\mathfrak{U}
is infinite. Now,

\displaystyle 1-Y_{p}

\displaystyle=\mathds{P}{}\left(\mathfrak{U}=\mathfrak{U}_{p-1}\mid\mathscr{F}_{p-1}\right)=\mathds{P}{}\left(\mathfrak{U}=\mathfrak{U}_{p-1},p\geq P\mid\mathscr{F}_{p-1}\right)+\mathds{P}{}\left(\mathfrak{U}=\mathfrak{U}_{p-1},p<P\mid\mathscr{F}_{p-1}\right)

\displaystyle=\mathds{P}{}\left(\mathfrak{F}\cap\mathfrak{K}_{p-1}=\varnothing,p\geq P\mid\mathscr{F}_{p-1}\right)+\mathds{P}{}\left(\mathfrak{U}=\mathfrak{U}_{p-1},p<P\mid\mathscr{F}_{p-1}\right)

\displaystyle=\mathds{P}{}\left(\mathfrak{F}\cap\mathfrak{K}_{p-1}=\varnothing\mid\mathscr{F}_{p-1}\right)-\mathds{P}{}\left(\mathfrak{F}\cap\mathfrak{K}_{p-1}=\varnothing,p<P\mid\mathscr{F}_{p-1}\right)

\displaystyle+\mathds{P}{}\left(\mathfrak{U}=\mathfrak{U}_{p-1},p<P\mid\mathscr{F}_{p-1}\right).

Observe that
\{\mathfrak{U}=\mathfrak{U}_{p-1}\}\subset\{\mathfrak{F}\cap\mathfrak{K}_{p-1}=\varnothing\},
since, by definition,
\mathfrak{K}_{p-1}
are the edges adjacent to both
\mathrm{C}_{p,0}
and
\mathfrak{U}_{p-1}
in
\mathrm{V}_{p}.
Thus,
1-Y_{p}=\mathds{P}{}\left(\mathfrak{F}\cap\mathfrak{K}_{p-1}=\varnothing\mid\mathscr{F}_{p-1}\right)-\mathds{P}{}\left(\mathfrak{F}\cap\mathfrak{K}_{p-1}=\varnothing,\mathfrak{U}\neq\mathfrak{U}_{p-1},p<P\mid\mathscr{F}_{p-1}\right).
If
\mathfrak{U}
grew at stage
p,
but it did not so through
\mathfrak{K}_{p-1},
then
\mathfrak{U}
grew through some edge adjacent to
\mathrm{E}_{p},
hence

\textstyle\mathds{P}{}\left(\mathfrak{F}\cap\mathfrak{K}_{p-1}=\varnothing,\mathfrak{U}\neq\mathfrak{U}_{p-1},p<P\mid\mathscr{F}_{p-1}\right)\leq\mathds{P}{}\left(\EuScript{E}_{p}\mid\mathscr{F}_{p-1}\right).

We know that
\mathds{P}{}\left(\EuScript{E}_{p}\right)\to 0,
so the sequence
(\mathds{P}{}\left(\EuScript{E}_{p}\mid\mathscr{F}_{p-1}\right))
converges to zero in
\mathscr{L}^{1}.
We may assume, passing through a subsequence should the need arise, that
(\mathds{P}{}\left(\EuScript{E}_{p}\mid\mathscr{F}_{p-1}\right))
converges to zero
\mathds{P}
-a.s. Therefore,
1-Y_{p}\geq\mathds{P}{}\left(\mathfrak{F}\cap\mathfrak{K}_{p-1}=\varnothing\mid\mathscr{F}_{p-1}\right)-\mathds{P}{}\left(\EuScript{E}_{p}\mid\mathscr{F}_{p-1}\right)
thus, on the event where
\mathfrak{U}
is an infinite component,
\mathds{P}{}\left(\mathfrak{F}\cap\mathfrak{K}_{p-1}=\varnothing\mid\mathscr{F}_{p-1}\right)\to 0.

Denote by
\mathfrak{G}_{p}
the random graph obtained from
\mathds{Z}^{d+1}
where all edges
e\in\mathfrak{U}_{p}
are shorted and the edges
e
in
\mathrm{V}_{p}
that are not in
\mathfrak{U}_{p}
are cut (
p\in\mathds{N}
). Elementary properties of wired spanning forest measures show that
\mathds{P}{}\left(\mathfrak{F}\cap\mathfrak{K}_{p-1}=\varnothing\mid\mathscr{F}_{p-1}\right)=\mathsf{WSF}_{\mathfrak{G}_{p-1}}\left(\mathfrak{F}\cap\mathfrak{K}_{p-1}=\varnothing\right),
the right hand side meaning the
\mathsf{WSF}
measure on the random graph
\mathfrak{G}_{p-1};
in this graph however,
\mathfrak{K}_{p-1}
is not random, while
\mathfrak{F}
is its
\mathsf{WSF}
-distributed random object. We can apply
[
LP16
, Lemma 10.40]
and conclude

\textstyle\mathsf{WSF}_{\mathfrak{G}_{p-1}}\left(\mathfrak{F}\cap\mathfrak{K}_{p-1}=\varnothing\right)\geq\prod\limits_{e\in\mathfrak{K}_{p-1}}\frac{1}{1+\mu(e)\mathscr{R}_{\mathfrak{G}_{p-1}\setminus\mathfrak{K}_{p-1}}^{W}\left(e^{-},e^{+}\right)},

where
\mathscr{R}_{\mathrm{G}}^{W}\left(e^{-},e^{+}\right)
stands for the wired effective resistance between
e^{-}
and
e^{+}
in the network
\mathrm{G}.
We know that
\mu(e)\asymp e^{-\lambda p}
for
e\in\mathfrak{K}_{p-1}
and
\mathscr{R}_{\mathfrak{G}_{p-1}\setminus\mathfrak{K}_{p-1}}^{W}\left(e^{-},e^{+}\right)\leq\mathscr{R}_{\mathfrak{G}_{p-1}\setminus\mathfrak{K}_{p-1}}\left(e^{-},\infty\right)+\mathscr{R}_{\mathfrak{G}_{p-1}\setminus\mathfrak{K}_{p-1}}\left(e^{+},\infty\right).
The following lemma is crux in the proof and shows at once why
d\geq 3
is needed (which has not been needed so far).

Lemma ( 5.16.4
)

Let
d\geq 3.
For
e\in\mathfrak{K}_{p-1},
we have
\mathscr{R}_{\mathfrak{G}_{p-1}\setminus\mathfrak{K}_{p-1}}\left(e^{\pm},\infty\right)\asymp e^{\lambda p}
(any implicit constant being universal).

Proof of lemma.

By Thompson’s principle
\mathscr{R}_{\Gamma}\left(v,\infty\right)=\inf\limits_{\theta}\mathscr{E}\left(\theta\right),
where
\theta
runs on the sets of unit flows from
v
to
\infty.
Since
d\geq 3,
we have that the standard random walk of
\mathds{Z}^{d}
is transient and T. Lyon’s criterion
[
Lyo88
]
shows the existence of a unit flow
\theta_{0}
from
0
to infinity having finite energy in the graph
\mathds{Z}^{d}.
We know that the network
\mathfrak{G}_{p-1}\setminus\mathfrak{K}_{p-1}
contains the hyperplane
\mathrm{H}_{p}=\{-p\}\times\mathds{Z}^{d}.
Observe that
\mathrm{H}_{p}
as a subnetwork of
\mathfrak{G}_{p-1}\setminus\mathfrak{K}_{p-1}
has constant resistances equal to
e^{\lambda p}.
Translate
\theta_{0}
to
\mathrm{H}_{p}
in the obvious way and denote the new flow by
\theta_{p}.
Clearly,
\mathscr{E}_{\mathrm{H}_{p}}\left(\theta_{p}\right)=e^{\lambda p}\mathscr{E}_{\mathds{Z}^{d}}\left(\theta_{0}\right),
where
\mathscr{E}_{\mathrm{G}}\left(\theta\right)
is the energy of the flow
\theta
on the network
\mathrm{G}.
Extend
\theta_{p}
to
\mathfrak{G}_{p-1}\setminus\mathfrak{K}_{p-1}
by sending zero flow through the edges outside
\mathrm{H}_{p}.
The energy of the extension of
\theta_{p},
denoted by
\overline{\theta}_{p},
does not change, that is to say,
\mathscr{E}_{\mathrm{H}_{p}}\left(\theta_{p}\right)=\mathscr{E}_{\mathfrak{G}_{p-1}\setminus\mathfrak{K}_{p-1}}\left(\overline{\theta}_{p}\right).
Thus, we proved that there exists a
c=\mathscr{E}_{\mathds{Z}^{d}}\left(\theta_{0}\right)>0
such that for all
p\in\mathds{N}
and all edges
e\in\mathfrak{K}_{p-1},

\mathscr{R}_{\mathfrak{G}_{p-1}\setminus\mathfrak{K}_{p-1}}\left(e^{\pm},\infty\right)\leq ce^{\lambda p}.

The reverse inequality is not needed but we prove it for completeness. Here we use that
\mathscr{C}\left(v,\infty\right)=\mathscr{R}\left(v,\infty\right)^{-1}
and we bound from above the effective conductance. We apply Dirichlet’s principle. Consider a vertex
z=(-p,x)
and define
\varphi=\mathds{1}_{z}.
Then,
\mathscr{C}_{\mathfrak{G}_{p-1}\setminus\mathfrak{K}_{p-1}}\left(z,\infty\right)\leq\mathscr{D}_{\mathfrak{G}_{p-1}\setminus\mathfrak{K}_{p-1}}\left(\varphi\right)\leq\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}e\text{ and edge of }\Gamma_{d}(\lambda)\\
e^{-}=z\end{subarray}}\mu(e)\leq 2(d+1)e^{\lambda}e^{-\lambda p}.
Thus, for
c^{-1}=2(d+1)e^{\lambda}>0,
we have
\mathscr{R}_{\mathfrak{G}_{p-1}\setminus\mathfrak{K}_{p-1}}\left(z,\infty\right)\geq ce^{\lambda p}.

∎

We may continue with the proof of one endedness in
\Gamma_{d}(\lambda).
By virtue of the previous lemma, there exists a universal
\delta>0
such that
\mathds{P}
-a.s.
\mathsf{WSF}_{\mathfrak{G}_{p-1}}\left(\mathfrak{F}\cap\mathfrak{K}_{p-1}=\varnothing\right)\geq\delta^{\mathrm{card}\left(\mathfrak{K}_{p-1}\right)}.
On the event
\mathfrak{U}
is infinite, we proved that
\mathsf{WSF}_{\mathfrak{G}_{p-1}}\left(\mathfrak{F}\cap\mathfrak{K}_{p-1}=\varnothing\right)\to 0
and so, on this event,
\mathrm{card}\left(\mathfrak{K}_{p-1}\right)\to\infty.

Consider then the random variables
Z_{p-1}=\mathrm{card}\left(\mathfrak{K}_{p-1}\right).
Notice that
(-p,x)\in\mathrm{C}_{p,0}
is adjacent to an edge of
\mathfrak{K}_{p-1}
if and only if
(-p+1,x)
is adjacent to an edge in
\mathfrak{U}_{p-1}.
Thus,

\textstyle\mathds{E}{}\left(Z_{p-1}\right)=\sum\limits_{z\in\mathrm{C}_{p-1,0}}\mathds{P}{}\left(z\text{ is connected to }0\text{ in }\mathfrak{U}_{p-1}\right)\leq\sum\limits_{z\in\mathrm{C}_{p-1,0}}\mathds{P}{}\left(z\text{ is connected to }0\text{ in }\mathfrak{F}\setminus L_{0}\right).

Using (
5.5.1
), we may calculate
\mathds{P}{}\left(z\text{ is connected to }0\text{ in }\mathfrak{F}\setminus L_{0}\right)
assuming that the order in which the vertices of
\mathds{Z}^{d+1}
were searched was
(0,z,\ldots).
Then, the event where
z
is connected to
0
in
\mathfrak{F}\setminus L_{0}
is the event where
S^{z}
visits
0
before hitting
L_{0}
anywhere else. In particular,
\mathds{E}{}\left(Z_{p-1}\right)\leq\sum\limits_{z\in\mathrm{C}_{p-1,0}}\mathds{P}{}\left(\tau_{0}(S^{z})<\infty\right)\leq\sum\limits_{z\in\mathrm{C}_{p-1,0}}G(z,0)\leq c
by (
5.16.1
). This implies
\mathds{P}{}\left(Z_{p}\to\infty\right)=0
(by Fatou’s lemma). Finally, we showed that
\mathds{P}{}\left(\mathrm{card}\left(\mathfrak{U}\right)=\infty\right)\leq\mathds{P}{}\left(Y_{p}\to 1\right)\leq\mathds{P}{}\left(Z_{p}\to\infty\right)=0.
The proof of (
5.16
) is complete by Lemma (
5.16.2
).
∎

5.5
On the rays of
\Gamma_{d}(\lambda)

We will say that a path
\gamma=(v_{j})_{j\in\mathds{Z}_{+}}
is
ultimately inside a region
\mathrm{R}
if there exists an almost surely finite random index
J
such that
v_{j}\in\mathrm{R}
for all
j\geq J.

Denote
\mathrm{P}=\left\{z=(n,x)\in\mathds{Z}\times\mathds{Z}^{d};\ \left\lVert x\right\rVert^{2}\leq n\right\}
and
\mathrm{P}^{\varepsilon}=\left\{z;\ \left\lVert x\right\rVert^{2}\leq n^{1+\varepsilon}\right\}.

Theorem ( 5.17
)

Let
z
be any vertex of
\Gamma_{d}(\lambda).
Then, there exists one and only one ray in
\mathsf{USF}
(
\mathsf{UST}
in
d=1,2
) starting at
z.
This ray is a random object (is
\mathsf{USF}
-measurable). Furthermore, for every
\varepsilon>0,
this ray is ultimately inside
z+\mathrm{P}^{\varepsilon},
for every
\varepsilon>0.

Proof.

By Theorems (
5.1
), (
5.15
) and (
5.16
) there exists one and only one ray, this proves at once such ray is
\mathsf{USF}
-measurable. There remains to prove that said ray is ultimately inside
z+\mathrm{P}^{\varepsilon}
for every
\varepsilon>0.
We may construct
\mathsf{UST}
in
d=1,2
or
\mathsf{USF}
for
d\geq 3
using Wilson’s algorithm rooted at infinity with the ordering of
\mathds{Z}^{d+1}
to be
(z,\ldots).
Thus, suffices to show that if
S^{z}
is the network random walk of
\Gamma_{d}(\lambda)
started at
z,
then
S^{z}
is ultimately inside
z+\mathrm{P}^{\varepsilon};
furthermore, by translation invariance, we may assume
z=0.

Let
S
denote the network random walk of
\Gamma_{d}(\lambda)
started at zero. To show that
S
is ultimately inside
\mathrm{P}
is the same as showing that, for every
\varepsilon>0,

S
will only be finitely many times outside
\mathrm{P}^{\varepsilon}.
We may apply Borel-Cantelli lemma to show this and as such, it all reduces to show that
\sum\limits_{n\in\mathds{Z}_{+}}\mathds{P}{}\left(S_{n}\notin\mathrm{P}^{\varepsilon}\right)<\infty.
The Green’s function estimates (
2.22
) give at once
\sum\limits_{n\in\mathds{Z}_{+}}\mathds{P}{}\left(S_{n}\notin\mathrm{P}^{\varepsilon}\right)\leq\sum\limits_{z\notin\mathrm{P}^{\varepsilon}}G(0,z)=\sum\limits_{z\in\mathrm{S}_{1}}G(0,z)+\sum\limits_{z\in\mathrm{S}_{2}\cap\mathrm{P}^{\varepsilon}}G(0,z),
where
\mathrm{S}_{1}=\{z;n\leq\left\lVert x\right\rVert\}
and
\mathrm{S}_{2}=\mathrm{S}_{1}^{\complement}.
Since
\sum\limits_{z\in\mathrm{S}_{1}}G(0,z)\leq\sum\limits_{z\in\mathds{Z}^{d+1}}e^{-c\left\lVert z\right\rVert}<\infty,
we focus on the second term. We have (write
z=(n,x)
)
\sum\limits_{z\in\mathrm{S}_{2}\cap\mathrm{P}^{\varepsilon}}G(0,z)\leq c_{1}\sum\limits_{n\in\mathds{Z}_{+}}\sum\limits_{n^{1+\varepsilon}\leq\left\lVert x\right\rVert^{2}\leq n^{2}}e^{-c_{2}\frac{\left\lVert x\right\rVert^{2}}{n}}\leq c\sum\limits_{n\in\mathds{Z}_{+}}e^{-c_{2}n^{\varepsilon}}n^{d}<\infty.

∎

6
Probability of belonging in the same component

In this section we will provide a function
u:\mathds{Z}^{d+1}\to\mathds{R}_{+}
with “polynomial decay” such that for all
z\in\mathds{Z}^{d+1},
the probability that
0
and
z
are in the same tree of
\Gamma_{d}(\lambda)
is
\asymp u(z);
the important part is that the decay depends solely on dimension. Recall that for
d=1
or
d=2,
on
\Gamma_{d}(\lambda)
we have a tree, thus the probability that
0
and any vertex
z
are connected is
1,
thus no decay.

6.1
The probability that two vertices are connected in the
\mathsf{USF}

As usual, we will write
z=(n,x),z^{\prime}=(n^{\prime},x^{\prime}),z_{1}=(n_{1},x_{1}),
etc. to denote the vertices of
\mathds{Z}^{d+1}=\mathds{Z}\times\mathds{Z}^{d}.
Define

(6.1)

\textstyle\eta(z)=\max\left(|n|^{\frac{1}{2}},\left\lVert x\right\rVert\right),\quad z=(n,x)\in\mathds{Z}^{d+1}.

This
\eta
has the following properties, all of which are obvious.

(a)

\eta(z)\geq 0,
and
\eta(z)=0
is equivalent to
z=0.

(b)

\eta(z)=\eta(-z);

(c)

\eta(z+z^{\prime})\leq\eta(z)+\eta(z^{\prime}).

Thus, the function
(z,z^{\prime})\mapsto\eta(z-z^{\prime})
is a metric on
\mathds{Z}^{d+1}.

Theorem ( 6.2
)

Let
d\geq 3.
Then,
\mathsf{USF}(0\text{ is connected to }z)\asymp\eta(z)^{-(d-2)},
with any constant depending solely on dimension.

Proof.

We remark that
\eta(z)^{-(d-2)}\asymp\max\left(n^{-\frac{d}{2}+1},\left\lVert x\right\rVert^{-(d-2)}\right),
and thus it suffices to show the bounds for any of these expressions. We also remark that for every
c>0,
there exists
c^{\prime}>0
such that
e^{-c\left\lVert z\right\rVert}\leq c^{\prime}\eta(z)^{-(d-2)};
thus, if at some point, we show an upper exponential bound in an estimate, we are done with that particular estimate.

Consider the event
\mathscr{C}(0,z)
that
0
is connected to
z
in
\mathsf{USF}.
Since the measure
\mathsf{USF}
is translation invariant in
\Gamma_{d}(\lambda),

\mathsf{USF}(\mathscr{C}(0,z))=\mathsf{USF}(\mathscr{C}(z^{\prime},z+z^{\prime}))
for any
z^{\prime}\in\mathds{Z}^{d+1}.
It is also clear that
\mathscr{C}(0,z)=\mathscr{C}(z,0).
Thus, we may assume
z\in\mathds{Z}_{+}\times\mathds{Z}^{d}.
Construct now
\mathsf{USF}
using Wilson’s algorithm rooted at infinity using the order
(0,z,\ldots)
of
\mathds{Z}^{d+1},
denote by
\mathfrak{F}
the random forest constructed in this way. Thus, we have on some probability space
(\Omega,\mathscr{F},\mathds{P})
a family of independent network random walks
(S^{v})_{v\in\mathds{Z}^{d+1}},
with
S^{v}
started at
v.
We know
\mathfrak{F}\sim\mathsf{USF},
and by construction
\left\{\mathfrak{F}\in\mathscr{C}(0,z)\right\}=\left\{S^{z}\cap\mathsf{LE}(S^{0}_{m})_{m\in\mathds{Z}_{+}}\neq\varnothing\right\}.
Thus, and with the aid of (
5.8
),

(A)

\textstyle\mathsf{USF}(\mathscr{C}(0,z))=\mathds{P}{}\left(S^{z}\cap\mathsf{LE}(S^{0}_{m})_{m\in\mathds{Z}_{+}}\neq\varnothing\right)\asymp\mathds{P}{}\left(S^{z}\cap S^{0}\neq\varnothing\right).

Denote by
K_{z}
the number of intersections between
S^{z}
and
S^{0}.
In other words,

(B)

\textstyle K_{z}=\sum\limits_{z^{\prime}\in\mathds{Z}^{d+1}}\sum\limits_{(p,q)\in\mathds{Z}_{+}^{2}}\mathds{1}_{\left\{S^{z}_{p}=z^{\prime},S^{0}_{q}=z^{\prime}\right\}}.

Clearly,
\mathds{P}{}\left(K_{z}>0\right)=\mathds{P}{}\left(S^{z}\cap S^{0}\neq\varnothing\right).
Independence and the Lebesgue-Tonelli theorem show at once

(C)

\textstyle\mathds{E}{}\left(K_{z}\right)=\sum\limits_{z^{\prime}\in\mathds{Z}^{d+1}}G(z,z^{\prime})G(0,z^{\prime}).

Lemma ( 6.2.1
)

We have
\mathds{P}{}\left(K_{z}>0\right)\asymp\mathds{E}{}\left(K_{z}\right),
where any implicit constant is universal (does not depend on
z
).

Proof of Lemma.

Since
K_{z}
is a
\mathds{Z}_{+}
-valued random variable, we have
\mathds{P}{}\left(K_{z}>0\right)\leq\mathds{E}{}\left(K_{z}\right).
There remains to prove a lower bound of the form
\mathds{P}{}\left(K_{z}>0\right)\geq c\mathds{E}{}\left(K_{z}\right),
for a universal constant
c>0.
Using the second moment inequality (
4.3
), suffices to establish that
\mathds{E}{}\left(K_{z}^{2}\right)\leq c\mathds{E}{}\left(K_{z}\right),
for some universal constant
c>0.
By definition,

\textstyle K_{z}^{2}=\sum\limits_{(z^{\prime},z^{\prime\prime})\in\mathds{Z}^{d+1}\times\mathds{Z}^{d+1}}\sum\limits_{(p,q,a,b)\in\mathds{Z}_{+}^{4}}\mathds{1}_{\left\{S^{z}_{p}=z^{\prime},S^{z}_{a}=z^{\prime\prime}\right\}}\mathds{1}_{\left\{S^{0}_{q}=z^{\prime},S^{0}_{b}=z^{\prime\prime}\right\}}.

By adding terms corresponding to
p=a
or
q=b,
then expanding the sum as to whether
p\leq a
or
p\geq a
and
q\leq b
or
q\geq b,
we reach a bound with four sums

\textstyle K_{z}^{2}\leq L_{1}+L_{2}+L_{3}+L_{4},

and

\displaystyle\mathds{E}{}\left(L_{1}\right)

\displaystyle\textstyle=\sum\limits_{(z^{\prime},z^{\prime\prime})\in\mathds{Z}^{d+1}\times\mathds{Z}^{d+1}}G(z,z^{\prime})G(z^{\prime},z^{\prime\prime})^{2}G(0,z^{\prime})

\displaystyle\mathds{E}{}\left(L_{2}\right)

\displaystyle\textstyle=\sum\limits_{(z^{\prime},z^{\prime\prime})\in\mathds{Z}^{d+1}\times\mathds{Z}^{d+1}}G(z,z^{\prime})G(z^{\prime},z^{\prime\prime})G(z^{\prime\prime},z^{\prime})G(0,z^{\prime\prime})

\displaystyle\mathds{E}{}\left(L_{3}\right)

\displaystyle\textstyle=\sum\limits_{(z^{\prime},z^{\prime\prime})\in\mathds{Z}^{d+1}\times\mathds{Z}^{d+1}}G(z,z^{\prime\prime})G(z^{\prime\prime},z^{\prime})G(z^{\prime},z^{\prime\prime})G(0,z^{\prime})

\displaystyle\mathds{E}{}\left(L_{4}\right)

\displaystyle\textstyle=\sum\limits_{(z^{\prime},z^{\prime\prime})\in\mathds{Z}^{d+1}\times\mathds{Z}^{d+1}}G(z,z^{\prime\prime})G(z^{\prime\prime},z^{\prime})^{2}G(0,z^{\prime\prime})

It is clear that
\mathds{E}{}\left(L_{1}\right)=\mathds{E}{}\left(L_{4}\right)
and
\mathds{E}{}\left(L_{2}\right)=\mathds{E}{}\left(L_{3}\right),
thus

\textstyle\mathds{E}{}\left(K_{z}^{2}\right)\leq 2\Big[\mathds{E}{}\left(L_{1}\right)+\mathds{E}{}\left(L_{2}\right)\Big]

We first handle the expectation of
L_{1},
we obtain

\textstyle\mathds{E}{}\left(L_{1}\right)=\sum\limits_{z^{\prime}\in\mathds{Z}^{d+1}}G(z,z^{\prime})G(0,z^{\prime})\sum\limits_{z^{\prime\prime}\in\mathds{Z}^{d+1}}G(z^{\prime},z^{\prime\prime})^{2}=\left[\sum\limits_{z^{\prime\prime}\in\mathds{Z}^{d+1}}G(0,z^{\prime\prime})^{2}\right]\sum\limits_{z^{\prime}\in\mathds{Z}^{d+1}}G(z,z^{\prime})G(0,z^{\prime}),

and
\sum\limits_{z^{\prime\prime}\in\mathds{Z}^{d+1}}G(0,z^{\prime\prime})^{2}
is a universal constant by the bubble condition (
3.10
).

We now handle
L_{2}.
First, the translation invariance of the Green’s function shows at once that

\textstyle\mathds{E}{}\left(L_{2}\right)=\sum\limits_{(z^{\prime},z^{\prime\prime})}G(z,z^{\prime})G(z^{\prime},z^{\prime\prime})G(z^{\prime\prime},z^{\prime})G(0,z^{\prime\prime})=\sum\limits_{z^{\prime}}G(z,z^{\prime})\sum\limits_{z^{\prime\prime}}G(0,z^{\prime\prime})G(z^{\prime\prime},0)G(0,z^{\prime}+z^{\prime\prime}).

In the following calculations we are going to establish that the inner sum in the line above, when viewed as a function of
z^{\prime},
possesses the same type of upper bounds as the Green’s function’s upper bounds (
2.22
). There are two cases to consider. Write
z^{\prime}=(n^{\prime},x^{\prime})
and similarly
z^{\prime\prime}=(n^{\prime\prime},x^{\prime\prime}).

Case 1.

Here we assume
\left\lVert x^{\prime}\right\rVert\leq n^{\prime}.
We then divide the inner sum into two, the first consists of all
z^{\prime\prime}
satisfying
|n^{\prime\prime}|\leq\frac{n^{\prime}}{2}
and
\left\lVert x^{\prime\prime}\right\rVert\leq\frac{\left\lVert x^{\prime}\right\rVert}{2}
and the second sum is over all other
z^{\prime\prime}.
In the first sum, we reach
\left\lVert x^{\prime}+x^{\prime\prime}\right\rVert\asymp\left\lVert x^{\prime}\right\rVert
and
|n^{\prime}+n^{\prime\prime}|\asymp n^{\prime}\asymp\left\lVert z^{\prime}\right\rVert,
then
G(0,z^{\prime}+z^{\prime\prime})\leq ce^{-c^{\prime}\frac{\left\lVert x^{\prime}\right\rVert^{2}}{n^{\prime}}}\left\lVert z^{\prime}\right\rVert^{-\frac{d}{2}}
for some constants
c,c^{\prime}>0.
This entails

\textstyle\sum\limits_{|n^{\prime\prime}|\leq\frac{n^{\prime}}{2},\left\lVert x^{\prime\prime}\right\rVert\leq\frac{\left\lVert x^{\prime}\right\rVert}{2}}G(z^{\prime\prime},0)G(0,z^{\prime\prime})G(0,z^{\prime}+z^{\prime\prime})\leq ce^{-c^{\prime}\frac{\left\lVert x^{\prime}\right\rVert^{2}}{n^{\prime}}}\left\lVert z^{\prime}\right\rVert^{-\frac{d}{2}}\sum\limits_{z^{\prime\prime}\in\mathds{Z}^{d+1}}G(z^{\prime\prime},0)G(0,z^{\prime\prime}),

for a pair of constants
c,c^{\prime}>0.
We may apply the inequality
ab\leq a^{2}+b^{2},
and obtain that
\sum\limits_{z^{\prime\prime}\in\mathds{Z}^{d+1}}G(z^{\prime\prime},0)G(0,z^{\prime\prime})<\infty
by the bubble condition (
3.10
). This shows that

\textstyle\sum\limits_{|n^{\prime\prime}|\leq\frac{n^{\prime}}{2},\left\lVert x^{\prime\prime}\right\rVert\leq\frac{\left\lVert x^{\prime}\right\rVert}{2}}G(z^{\prime\prime},0)G(0,z^{\prime\prime})G(0,z^{\prime}+z^{\prime\prime})\leq ce^{-c^{\prime}\frac{\left\lVert x^{\prime}\right\rVert^{2}}{n^{\prime}}}\left\lVert z^{\prime}\right\rVert^{-\frac{d}{2}},

for a pair of positive constants
c,c^{\prime}>0.
Next, we are going to handle the second sum, which is over all
z^{\prime\prime}
for which either
|n^{\prime\prime}|>\frac{n^{\prime}}{2}
or
\left\lVert x^{\prime\prime}\right\rVert>\frac{\left\lVert x^{\prime}\right\rVert}{2}.
Here, we will use that one of the two factors
G(z^{\prime\prime},0),

G(0,z^{\prime\prime})
has an upper bound of the form
e^{-c\left\lVert z^{\prime\prime}\right\rVert}.
We will write
e^{-c\left\lVert z^{\prime\prime}\right\rVert}=e^{-\frac{c}{2}\left\lVert z^{\prime\prime}\right\rVert}e^{-\frac{c}{2}\left\lVert z^{\prime\prime}\right\rVert}
and use the fact that the Green’s function is bounded by some constant
L>0.
Since
\left\lVert z^{\prime}\right\rVert\asymp n^{\prime},
we may write

\textstyle\sum\limits_{|n^{\prime\prime}|>\frac{n^{\prime}}{2},x^{\prime\prime}\in\mathds{Z}^{d}}G(z^{\prime\prime},0)G(0,z^{\prime\prime})G(0,z^{\prime}+z^{\prime\prime})\leq L^{2}e^{-\frac{c}{4}n^{\prime}}\sum\limits_{z^{\prime\prime}\in\mathds{Z}^{d+1}}e^{-\frac{c}{2}\left\lVert z^{\prime\prime}\right\rVert}\leq c^{\prime}e^{-c^{\prime\prime}\left\lVert z^{\prime}\right\rVert},

for positive constants
c,c^{\prime},c^{\prime\prime}>0.
It is clear that
c^{\prime}e^{-c^{\prime\prime}\left\lVert z^{\prime}\right\rVert}\leq Ce^{-C^{\prime}\frac{\left\lVert x^{\prime}\right\rVert^{2}}{n^{\prime}}}\left\lVert z^{\prime}\right\rVert^{-\frac{d}{2}}
for another pair of constants
C,C^{\prime}>0.
We finally deal with the sum corresponding to
|n^{\prime\prime}|\leq\frac{n^{\prime}}{2}
and
\left\lVert x^{\prime\prime}\right\rVert>\frac{\left\lVert x^{\prime}\right\rVert}{2}.
Here we decompose again
e^{-c\left\lVert z^{\prime\prime}\right\rVert}=\left(e^{-\frac{c}{2}\left\lVert z^{\prime\prime}\right\rVert}\right)^{2}.
The assumption
\left\lVert x^{\prime}\right\rVert\leq n^{\prime}
shows that
\left\lVert x^{\prime}\right\rVert\geq\frac{\left\lVert x^{\prime}\right\rVert^{2}}{n^{\prime}},
and thus

\textstyle e^{-\frac{c}{2}\left\lVert z^{\prime\prime}\right\rVert}\leq e^{-\frac{c}{2}\left\lVert x^{\prime\prime}\right\rVert}\leq e^{-\frac{c}{4}\left\lVert x^{\prime}\right\rVert}\leq e^{-\frac{c}{6}\frac{\left\lVert x^{\prime}\right\rVert^{2}}{n^{\prime}}}.

Also, it is clear that
G(0,z^{\prime}+z^{\prime\prime})\leq c^{\prime}\left\lVert z^{\prime}\right\rVert^{-\frac{d}{2}}
for some positive constant
c^{\prime}>0
since
\left\lVert z^{\prime}+z^{\prime\prime}\right\rVert\asymp\left\lVert z^{\prime}\right\rVert.
Thus,

\textstyle\sum\limits_{|n^{\prime\prime}|\leq\frac{n^{\prime}}{2},\left\lVert x^{\prime\prime}\right\rVert>\frac{\left\lVert x^{\prime}\right\rVert}{2}}G(z^{\prime\prime},0)G(0,z^{\prime\prime})G(0,z^{\prime}+z^{\prime\prime})\leq c^{\prime}e^{-\frac{c}{6}\frac{\left\lVert x^{\prime}\right\rVert^{2}}{n^{\prime}}}\left\lVert z^{\prime}\right\rVert^{-\frac{d}{2}},

for a pair of constants.

Case 2.

Here we assume
|n^{\prime}|<\left\lVert x^{\prime}\right\rVert.
Split
\sum\limits_{z^{\prime\prime}}G(z^{\prime\prime},0)G(0,z^{\prime\prime})G(0,z^{\prime}+z^{\prime\prime})=A+B,
where
A
is the sum corresponding to all
z^{\prime\prime}=(n^{\prime\prime},x^{\prime\prime})
satisfying
|n^{\prime\prime}|\leq\frac{\left\lVert x^{\prime}\right\rVert}{2}
and
\left\lVert x^{\prime\prime}\right\rVert\leq\frac{\left\lVert x^{\prime}\right\rVert}{2},

B
is the sum over all other
z^{\prime\prime}.
For
z^{\prime\prime}
in the range of
A,
we obtain
\left\lVert x^{\prime}+x^{\prime\prime}\right\rVert\asymp\left\lVert x^{\prime}\right\rVert\asymp\left\lVert z^{\prime}\right\rVert
and
|n^{\prime}+n^{\prime\prime}|\leq c\left\lVert x^{\prime}\right\rVert\leq c\left\lVert z^{\prime}\right\rVert,
and this shows that, on this range,
G(0,z^{\prime}+z^{\prime\prime})\leq ce^{-c^{\prime}\left\lVert z^{\prime}\right\rVert}
for a pair of constants
c,c^{\prime}>0.
Then, there exists two universal constants
c,c^{\prime}>0
such that (recall from
Case 1
that
G(z^{\prime\prime},0)G(0,z^{\prime\prime})
is a summable family for
z^{\prime\prime}\in\mathds{Z}^{d+1}
)
A\leq ce^{-c^{\prime}\left\lVert z^{\prime}\right\rVert}.
In the range of
B,
we have that either
|n^{\prime\prime}|>\frac{\left\lVert x^{\prime}\right\rVert}{2}\asymp\left\lVert z^{\prime}\right\rVert
or
\left\lVert x^{\prime\prime}\right\rVert>\frac{\left\lVert x^{\prime}\right\rVert}{2}\asymp\left\lVert z^{\prime}\right\rVert,
thus
\left\lVert z^{\prime\prime}\right\rVert\geq c\left\lVert z^{\prime}\right\rVert
for a universal constant
c>0.
Next, one of the two factor
G(z^{\prime\prime},0)
or
G(0,z^{\prime\prime})
is bounded from above by
e^{-c\left\lVert z^{\prime\prime}\right\rVert}
by (
2.22
). Let
L>0
be a bound for the Green’s function of
\Gamma_{d}(\lambda).
Then
B\leq L^{2}e^{-c^{\prime}\left\lVert z^{\prime}\right\rVert}\sum\limits_{z^{\prime\prime}\in\mathds{Z}^{d+1}}e^{-\frac{c}{2}\left\lVert z^{\prime\prime}\right\rVert}\leq Ce^{-c^{\prime}\left\lVert z^{\prime}\right\rVert}.

By virtue of the two cases above, we have established that there exists two constants
c,c^{\prime}>0
such that (write
z^{\prime}=(n^{\prime},x^{\prime})
)

\textstyle\sum\limits_{z^{\prime\prime}\in\mathds{Z}^{d+1}}G(z^{\prime\prime},0)G(0,z^{\prime\prime})G(z^{\prime}+z^{\prime\prime})\leq\begin{cases}ce^{-c^{\prime}\left\lVert z^{\prime}\right\rVert}&\text{ if }\left\lVert x^{\prime}\right\rVert>n^{\prime},\\
ce^{-c^{\prime}\frac{\left\lVert x^{\prime}\right\rVert^{2}}{n^{\prime}}}\left\lVert z^{\prime}\right\rVert^{-\frac{d}{2}}&\text{ if }\left\lVert x^{\prime}\right\rVert\leq n^{\prime}.\end{cases}

The proof of (
6.2.1
) is now an immediate consequence of the next lemma. Indeed, the next lemma shows at once that
\mathds{E}{}\left(K_{z}\right)\asymp\eta(z)^{-(d-2)}
and that
\mathds{E}{}\left(L_{2}\right)\leq c\eta(z)^{-(d-2)}.
Therefore,
\mathds{E}{}\left(K_{z}^{2}\right)\leq c\eta(z)^{-(d-2)}\leq c^{\prime}\mathds{E}{}\left(K_{z}\right),
as wanted.
∎

Lemma ( 6.2.2
)

Suppose that
\varphi
and
\psi
are two bounded functions defined on
\mathds{Z}^{d+1}
and with positive values. Furthermore, suppose that there exists two constants
c_{1},c_{2}>0
such that

\textstyle\max(\varphi(z),\psi(z))\leq\begin{cases}c_{1}e^{-c_{2}\left\lVert z\right\rVert}&\text{ if }\left\lVert x\right\rVert>n,\\
c_{1}e^{-c_{2}\frac{\left\lVert x\right\rVert^{2}}{n}}\left\lVert z\right\rVert^{-\frac{d}{2}}&\text{ if }\left\lVert x\right\rVert\leq n.\end{cases}

Then, there exists a universal constant
c>0
such that for any
z\in\mathds{Z}^{d+1},

\sum\limits_{z^{\prime}\in\mathds{Z}^{d+1}}\varphi(z^{\prime}-z)\psi(z^{\prime})\leq c\eta(z)^{-(d-2)}.
Likewise, if there exists two constants
c_{3},c_{4}>0
such that

\textstyle\min(\varphi(z),\psi(z))\geq\begin{cases}c_{3}e^{-c_{4}\left\lVert z\right\rVert}&\text{ if }\left\lVert x\right\rVert>n,\\
c_{3}e^{-c_{4}\frac{\left\lVert x\right\rVert^{2}}{n}}\left\lVert z\right\rVert^{-\frac{d}{2}}&\text{ if }\left\lVert x\right\rVert\leq n,\end{cases}

then there exists a universal constant
c>0
such that for any
z\in\mathds{Z}^{d+1},

\sum\limits_{z^{\prime}\in\mathds{Z}^{d+1}}\varphi(z^{\prime}-z)\psi(z^{\prime})\geq c\eta(z)^{-(d-2)}.

Proof of Lemma.

We divide the proof of the upper and lower bounds. We write
z=(n,x),

z^{\prime}=(n^{\prime},x^{\prime})
and so on with other affixes (such as superscripts).

I. Upper bound.

There is no loss of generality to assume
\varphi=\psi.
Decompose
\sum\limits_{z^{\prime}\in\mathds{Z}^{d+1}}\varphi(z^{\prime}-z)\varphi(z^{\prime})=H+I+J,
where

(D)

\textstyle H=\sum\limits_{n^{\prime}\leq 0}\sum\limits_{x^{\prime}\in\mathds{Z}^{d}}\varphi(z^{\prime}-z)\varphi(z^{\prime}),\quad I=\sum\limits_{1\leq n^{\prime}\leq n-1}\sum\limits_{x^{\prime}\in\mathds{Z}^{d}}\varphi(z^{\prime}-z)\varphi(z^{\prime}),\quad J=\sum\limits_{n^{\prime}\geq n}\sum\limits_{x^{\prime}\in\mathds{Z}^{d}}\varphi(z^{\prime}-z)\varphi(z^{\prime})

We shall bound now each of the terms
H,I,J
and we will see, at the end, that the largest of these bounds is of the claimed form. We will use the bounds in the hypothesis and the estimates of §
2.3
to handle the resulting sums.

We start by bounding
H.
Since
z\in\mathds{Z}_{+}\times\mathds{Z}^{d}
and
z^{\prime}\in\mathds{Z}_{-}\times\mathds{Z}^{d},
we have the bounds
\varphi(z^{\prime}-z)\varphi(z^{\prime})\leq ce^{-c^{\prime}(\left\lVert z-z^{\prime}\right\rVert+\left\lVert z^{\prime}\right\rVert)},
for a pair of positive constants. Now,

\textstyle ce^{-c^{\prime}(\left\lVert z-z^{\prime}\right\rVert+\left\lVert z^{\prime}\right\rVert)}\leq ce^{-\frac{c^{\prime}}{2}(\left\lVert z\right\rVert-\left\lVert z^{\prime}\right\rVert)-c^{\prime}\left\lVert z^{\prime}\right\rVert}\leq ce^{-\frac{c^{\prime}}{2}\left\lVert z\right\rVert}e^{-\frac{c^{\prime}}{2}\left\lVert z^{\prime}\right\rVert},

summing over
z^{\prime}\in\mathds{Z}_{-}\times\mathds{Z}^{d},
we reach

(E)

\textstyle H\leq ce^{-c^{\prime}\left\lVert z\right\rVert},

for a pair of constants
c,c^{\prime}>0.

We now handle
I
from (
D
). Divide into two parts as follows

\textstyle I=I_{1}+I_{2}\mathop{=}\limits^{\mathrm{def.}}\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq n^{\prime}\leq n-1\\
\left\lVert x^{\prime}\right\rVert\leq n^{\prime}\end{subarray}}\varphi(z^{\prime}-z)\varphi(z^{\prime})+\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq n^{\prime}\leq n-1\\
\left\lVert x^{\prime}\right\rVert>n^{\prime}\end{subarray}}\varphi(z^{\prime}-z)\varphi(z^{\prime}).

Then,
I_{1}\leq c\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq n^{\prime}\leq n-1\\
\left\lVert x^{\prime}\right\rVert\leq n^{\prime}\end{subarray}}\exp\left(-c^{\prime}\left(\left\lVert z-z^{\prime}\right\rVert+\frac{\left\lVert x^{\prime}\right\rVert^{2}}{n^{\prime}}\right)\right)(n^{\prime})^{-\frac{d}{2}}.
Notice
\left\lVert z-z^{\prime}\right\rVert^{2}=(n-n^{\prime})^{2}+\left\lVert x-x^{\prime}\right\rVert^{2}\geq\frac{\left(|n-n^{\prime}|+\left\lVert x-x^{\prime}\right\rVert\right)^{2}}{2},
thus
\left\lVert z-z^{\prime}\right\rVert\geq\frac{|n-n^{\prime}|+\left\lVert x-x^{\prime}\right\rVert}{\sqrt{2}}.
Hence,

(F)

\textstyle I_{1}\leq c\sum\limits_{n^{\prime}=1}^{n-1}(n^{\prime})^{-\frac{d}{2}}e^{-\frac{c^{\prime}}{\sqrt{2}}(n-n^{\prime})}\sum\limits_{\left\lVert x^{\prime}\right\rVert\leq n^{\prime}}\exp\left(-\frac{c^{\prime}}{\sqrt{2}}\left(\left\lVert x-x^{\prime}\right\rVert+\frac{\left\lVert x^{\prime}\right\rVert^{2}}{n^{\prime}}\right)\right).

For convenience, we now divide into two cases. But first, by (
2.12
), we can bound

\textstyle\sum\limits_{\left\lVert x^{\prime}\right\rVert\leq n^{\prime}}\exp\left(-\frac{c^{\prime}}{\sqrt{2}}\left(\left\lVert x-x^{\prime}\right\rVert+\frac{\left\lVert x^{\prime}\right\rVert^{2}}{n^{\prime}}\right)\right)\leq\begin{cases}ce^{-c^{\prime}\left\lVert x\right\rVert}&\text{ if }\left\lVert x\right\rVert>n^{\prime}\\
ce^{-c^{\prime}\frac{\left\lVert x\right\rVert^{2}}{n^{\prime}}}&\text{ if }\left\lVert x\right\rVert\leq n^{\prime}.\end{cases}

We may now proceed with the two cases.

Case 1.

Here we assume
n\leq\left\lVert x\right\rVert.
Then, there exists two constants
c,c^{\prime}>0,

\textstyle I_{1}\leq ce^{-c^{\prime}\left\lVert x\right\rVert}\sum\limits_{n^{\prime}=1}^{n-1}(n^{\prime})^{-\frac{d}{2}}e^{-c^{\prime}(n-n^{\prime})}\leq c\left[\sum\limits_{k=1}^{\infty}k^{-\frac{d}{2}}\right]e^{-c^{\prime}\left\lVert x\right\rVert}\leq Ce^{-C^{\prime}\left\lVert z\right\rVert}.

Case 2.

Here we assume
\left\lVert x\right\rVert<n.
Then, we divide the sum in the right of (
F
) into two parts. The first part consists on all terms for which
1\leq n^{\prime}\leq\left\lVert x\right\rVert
and the second,
\left\lVert x\right\rVert<n^{\prime}\leq n-1.
Then, for a pair of constants
C,C^{\prime}>0,

\textstyle\sum\limits_{1\leq n^{\prime}\leq\left\lVert x\right\rVert}(n^{\prime})^{-\frac{d}{2}}e^{-\frac{c^{\prime}}{\sqrt{2}}(n-n^{\prime})}\sum\limits_{\left\lVert x^{\prime}\right\rVert\leq n^{\prime}}e^{-\frac{c^{\prime}}{\sqrt{2}}\left(\left\lVert x-x^{\prime}\right\rVert+\frac{\left\lVert x^{\prime}\right\rVert^{2}}{n^{\prime}}\right)}\leq C\sum\limits_{1\leq n^{\prime}\leq\left\lVert x\right\rVert}(n^{\prime})^{-\frac{d}{2}}e^{-\frac{c^{\prime}}{\sqrt{2}}(n-n^{\prime})}e^{-C^{\prime}\left\lVert x\right\rVert}.

By setting
c^{\prime\prime}=\min\left(\frac{c^{\prime}}{\sqrt{2}},C^{\prime}\right),
it is clear that
e^{-\frac{c^{\prime}}{\sqrt{2}}(n-n^{\prime})}e^{-C^{\prime}\left\lVert x\right\rVert}\leq e^{-c^{\prime\prime}(n-n^{\prime}+\left\lVert x\right\rVert)}\leq e^{-c^{\prime\prime}n},
for
1\leq n^{\prime}\leq\left\lVert x\right\rVert.
Then, the first part in the division of
I_{1}
is bounded above by
ce^{-c^{\prime}\left\lVert z\right\rVert},
for a pair of constants
c,c^{\prime}>0.
As for the second part,

\displaystyle\textstyle\sum\limits_{\left\lVert x\right\rVert<n^{\prime}\leq n-1}(n^{\prime})^{-\frac{d}{2}}e^{-\frac{c^{\prime}}{\sqrt{2}}(n-n^{\prime})}

\displaystyle\textstyle\displaystyle\sum\limits_{\left\lVert x^{\prime}\right\rVert\leq n^{\prime}}e^{-\frac{c^{\prime}}{\sqrt{2}}\left(\left\lVert x-x^{\prime}\right\rVert+\frac{\left\lVert x^{\prime}\right\rVert^{2}}{n^{\prime}}\right)}

\displaystyle\textstyle\displaystyle\leq C\sum\limits_{\left\lVert x\right\rVert<n^{\prime}\leq n-1}(n^{\prime})^{-\frac{d}{2}}e^{-\frac{c^{\prime}}{\sqrt{2}}(n-n^{\prime})}e^{-C^{\prime}\frac{\left\lVert x\right\rVert^{2}}{n^{\prime}}},

where
C,C^{\prime}>0
are two constants. We have shown up to this point that under the assumption
\left\lVert x\right\rVert<n,
there exists two constants
c,c^{\prime}>0,
such that

(G)

\textstyle I_{1}\leq ce^{-c^{\prime}\left\lVert z\right\rVert}+c\sum\limits_{\left\lVert x\right\rVert<n^{\prime}\leq n-1}(n^{\prime})^{-\frac{d}{2}}e^{-c^{\prime}\left(n-n^{\prime}+\frac{\left\lVert x\right\rVert^{2}}{n^{\prime}}\right)}.

We are going to consider two further cases for the sum appearing in the previous line.

(a)

Assume first
\left\lVert x\right\rVert\leq n^{\frac{1}{2}}.
Here, we will bound
e^{-c\frac{\left\lVert x\right\rVert^{2}}{n^{\prime}}}\leq 1.
Then,

\textstyle\sum\limits_{\left\lVert x\right\rVert<n^{\prime}\leq n-1}(n^{\prime})^{-\frac{d}{2}}e^{-c^{\prime}\left(n-n^{\prime}+\frac{\left\lVert x\right\rVert^{2}}{n^{\prime}}\right)}=\sum\limits_{\left\lVert x\right\rVert<n^{\prime}\leq n^{\frac{1}{2}}}(n^{\prime})^{-\frac{d}{2}}e^{-c^{\prime}\left(n-n^{\prime}\right)}+\sum\limits_{n^{\frac{1}{2}}<n^{\prime}\leq n}(n^{\prime})^{-\frac{d}{2}}e^{-c^{\prime}\left(n-n^{\prime}\right)}.

In the first of these sums,
e^{-c^{\prime}(n-n^{\prime})}\leq e^{-c^{\prime}\left(n-n^{\frac{1}{2}}\right)}\leq Ce^{-C^{\prime}n^{\frac{1}{2}}},
for two constants
C,C^{\prime}>0.
We reach
\sum\limits_{\left\lVert x\right\rVert<n^{\prime}\leq n^{\frac{1}{2}}}(n^{\prime})^{-\frac{d}{2}}e^{-c^{\prime}\left(n-n^{\prime}\right)}\leq C\left[\sum\limits_{k=1}^{\infty}k^{-\frac{d}{2}}\right]e^{-C^{\prime}n^{\frac{1}{2}}}.
Observe now that if
n^{\frac{1}{2}}<n^{\prime}\leq n,
there exists a unique integer
j
between
1
and
n^{\frac{1}{2}}
such that
jn^{\frac{1}{2}}<n^{\prime}\leq(j+1)n^{\frac{1}{2}}
(there are
n^{\frac{1}{2}}
indices
n^{\prime}
in this range). Thus,

\displaystyle\textstyle\sum\limits_{n^{\frac{1}{2}}<n^{\prime}\leq n}(n^{\prime})^{-\frac{d}{2}}e^{-c^{\prime}\left(n-n^{\prime}\right)}

\displaystyle\textstyle\leq n^{-\frac{d}{4}}n^{\frac{1}{2}}\sum\limits_{1\leq j\leq n^{\frac{1}{2}}}j^{-\frac{d}{2}}e^{-c^{\prime}\left(n-\min\left((j+1)n^{\frac{1}{2}},n\right)\right)}

\displaystyle\textstyle=n^{-\frac{d}{4}+\frac{1}{2}}\sum\limits_{1\leq j\leq n^{\frac{1}{2}}}j^{-\frac{d}{2}}e^{-c^{\prime}n^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(n^{\frac{1}{2}}-\min\left(j+1,n^{\frac{1}{2}}\right)\right)}.

In the last sum, consider first the range
1\leq j\leq\frac{n^{\frac{1}{2}}}{4}
to obtain
j+1\leq 2j\leq\frac{n^{\frac{1}{2}}}{2},
giving
e^{-c^{\prime}n^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(n^{\frac{1}{2}}-\min\left(j+1,n^{\frac{1}{2}}\right)\right)}\leq e^{-\frac{c^{\prime}}{2}n}.
Next, consider the range
\frac{n^{\frac{1}{2}}}{4}<j\leq n^{\frac{1}{2}},
so that
j\asymp n^{\frac{1}{2}}.
Then,

\textstyle\sum\limits_{n^{\frac{1}{2}}<n^{\prime}\leq n}(n^{\prime})^{-\frac{d}{2}}e^{-c^{\prime}\left(n-n^{\prime}\right)}\leq\left[\sum\limits_{k=1}^{\infty}k^{-\frac{d}{2}}\right]n^{-\frac{d}{4}+\frac{1}{2}}e^{-\frac{c^{\prime}}{2}n}+cn^{-\frac{d}{4}+\frac{1}{2}}n^{-\frac{d}{4}}n^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq Cn^{-\frac{d}{2}+1}.

The foregoing shows
\sum\limits_{\left\lVert x\right\rVert\leq n^{\prime}<n-1}(n^{\prime})^{-\frac{d}{2}}e^{-c^{\prime}\left(n-n^{\prime}+\frac{\left\lVert x\right\rVert^{2}}{n^{\prime}}\right)}\leq ce^{-c^{\prime}n}+Cn^{-\frac{d}{2}+1}\leq C^{\prime\prime}n^{-\frac{d}{2}+1}.

(b)

Now assume
n^{\frac{1}{2}}<\left\lVert x\right\rVert.
We bound the sum appearing in (
G
). We use (
2.14
),

\textstyle\sum\limits_{\left\lVert x\right\rVert<n^{\prime}\leq n-1}(n^{\prime})^{-\frac{d}{2}}e^{-c^{\prime}\left(n-n^{\prime}+\frac{\left\lVert x\right\rVert^{2}}{n^{\prime}}\right)}\leq\sum\limits_{n^{\prime}\leq\left\lVert x\right\rVert^{2}}(n^{\prime})^{-\frac{d}{2}}e^{-c^{\prime}\frac{\left\lVert x\right\rVert^{2}}{n^{\prime}}}\leq c\left\lVert x\right\rVert^{-(d-2)}.

Bearing in mind the previous two items, and (
G
), we have established that
I_{1}\leq c\eta(z)^{-(d-2)},
for a universal constant
c>0.
This completes
Case 2.

We now need to bound
I_{2}.
Again, by the bounds in the hypothesis,

\textstyle I_{2}=\sum\limits_{1\leq n^{\prime}\leq n-1}\sum\limits_{\left\lVert x^{\prime}\right\rVert>n^{\prime}}\varphi(z^{\prime}-z)\varphi(z^{\prime})\leq c_{1}^{2}\sum\limits_{1\leq n^{\prime}\leq n-1}\sum\limits_{\left\lVert x^{\prime}\right\rVert>n^{\prime}}e^{-c_{2}(\left\lVert z-z^{\prime}\right\rVert+\left\lVert z^{\prime}\right\rVert)}\leq ce^{-c^{\prime}\left\lVert z\right\rVert},

where we proceeded as in (
E
). Thus, summing the upper bounds of
I_{1}
and
I_{2}
we have shown that

(H)

\textstyle I\leq c\eta(z)^{-(d-2)},

for a universal constant
c>0,
as desired.

We now proceed to upper bound the sum
J
of (
D
). First we rewrite,
J=\sum\limits_{n^{\prime}\geq 0}\sum\limits_{x^{\prime}\in\mathds{Z}^{d}}\varphi(0,z^{\prime})\varphi(0,z^{\prime}+z)=J_{1}+J_{2},
where
J_{1}=\sum\limits_{n^{\prime}\geq 0}\sum\limits_{\left\lVert x^{\prime}\right\rVert\leq n^{\prime}}\varphi(0,z^{\prime})\varphi(0,z^{\prime}+z)
and
J_{2}=\sum\limits_{n^{\prime}\geq 0}\sum\limits_{\left\lVert x^{\prime}\right\rVert>n^{\prime}}\varphi(0,z^{\prime})\varphi(0,z^{\prime}+z).
We further divide
J_{1}
as follows:
J_{1}=J_{1,1}+J_{1,2},
with
J_{1,1}=\sum\limits_{n^{\prime}\geq 0}\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\left\lVert x^{\prime}\right\rVert\leq n^{\prime}\\
\left\lVert x+x^{\prime}\right\rVert\leq n+n^{\prime}\end{subarray}}\varphi(0,z^{\prime})\varphi(0,z^{\prime}+z)
and
J_{1,2}=\sum\limits_{n^{\prime}\geq 0}\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\left\lVert x^{\prime}\right\rVert\leq n^{\prime}\\
\left\lVert x+x^{\prime}\right\rVert>n+n^{\prime}\end{subarray}}\varphi(0,z^{\prime})\varphi(0,z^{\prime}+z).
Let us handle
J_{1,1}
first. In this case, we have the bound

\textstyle J_{1,1}\leq c_{1}^{2}\sum\limits_{n^{\prime}\geq 0}(n^{\prime})^{-\frac{d}{2}}(n+n^{\prime})^{-\frac{d}{2}}\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\left\lVert x^{\prime}\right\rVert\leq n^{\prime}\\
\left\lVert x+x^{\prime}\right\rVert\leq n+n^{\prime}\end{subarray}}e^{-c_{2}\left(\frac{\left\lVert x^{\prime}\right\rVert^{2}}{n^{\prime}}+\frac{\left\lVert x+x^{\prime}\right\rVert^{2}}{n+n^{\prime}}\right)}.

We finish the case
\left\lVert x\right\rVert\leq n^{\frac{1}{2}}
first. Here we may bound

\textstyle J_{1,1}\leq c_{1}^{2}\sum\limits_{n^{\prime}\geq 0}(n^{\prime})^{-\frac{d}{2}}(n+n^{\prime})^{-\frac{d}{2}}\sum\limits_{\left\lVert x^{\prime}\right\rVert\leq n^{\prime}}e^{-c_{2}\frac{\left\lVert x^{\prime}\right\rVert^{2}}{n^{\prime}}}\leq c\sum\limits_{n^{\prime}\geq 0}(n+n^{\prime})^{-\frac{d}{2}}\leq c^{\prime}n^{-\frac{d}{2}+1},

by (
2.11
) and (
2.13
) with all
c,c^{\prime},c^{\prime\prime}
positive constants. We may assume for the rest of the bounding of
J_{1,1}
that
\left\lVert x\right\rVert>n^{\frac{1}{2}}.
By virtue of (
2.15
), in the range
0\leq n^{\prime}\leq\frac{\left\lVert x\right\rVert}{2},
we may bound

\textstyle\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\left\lVert x^{\prime}\right\rVert\leq n^{\prime}\\
\left\lVert x+x^{\prime}\right\rVert\leq n+n^{\prime}\end{subarray}}e^{-c_{2}\left(\frac{\left\lVert x^{\prime}\right\rVert^{2}}{n^{\prime}}+\frac{\left\lVert x+x^{\prime}\right\rVert^{2}}{n+n^{\prime}}\right)}\leq c(n^{\prime})^{\frac{d}{2}}e^{-c\frac{\left\lVert x\right\rVert^{2}}{n+n^{\prime}}},

with
c
a universal constant. Next,

\textstyle\sum\limits_{0\leq n^{\prime}\leq\frac{\left\lVert x\right\rVert}{2}}(n^{\prime})^{-\frac{d}{2}}(n+n^{\prime})^{-\frac{d}{2}}(n^{\prime})^{\frac{d}{2}}e^{-c^{\prime}\frac{\left\lVert x\right\rVert^{2}}{n+n^{\prime}}}=c\sum\limits_{0\leq n^{\prime}\leq\frac{\left\lVert x\right\rVert}{2}}(n+n^{\prime})^{-\frac{d}{2}}e^{-c^{\prime}\frac{\left\lVert x\right\rVert^{2}}{n+n^{\prime}}}=c\sum\limits_{n\leq m\leq n+\frac{\left\lVert x\right\rVert}{2}}m^{-\frac{d}{2}}e^{-c^{\prime}\frac{\left\lVert x\right\rVert^{2}}{m}}.

By the assumption
\left\lVert x\right\rVert>n^{\frac{1}{2}},
we have
\sum\limits_{n\leq m\leq n+\frac{\left\lVert x\right\rVert}{2}}m^{-\frac{d}{2}}e^{-c^{\prime}\frac{\left\lVert x\right\rVert^{2}}{m}}\leq\sum\limits_{m\leq 2\left\lVert x\right\rVert^{2}}m^{-\frac{d}{2}}e^{-c^{\prime}\frac{\left\lVert x\right\rVert^{2}}{m}}\leq c\left\lVert x\right\rVert^{-(d-2)},
by (
2.14
). To sum up, we have established so far in the bounding of
J_{1,1}
(with the assumption that
\left\lVert x\right\rVert>n^{\frac{1}{2}}
),

\textstyle J_{1,1}\leq c\eta(z)^{-(d-2)}+c\sum\limits_{n^{\prime}\geq\frac{\left\lVert x\right\rVert}{2}}(n^{\prime})^{-\frac{d}{2}}(n+n^{\prime})^{-\frac{d}{2}}\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\left\lVert x^{\prime}\right\rVert\leq n^{\prime}\\
\left\lVert x+x^{\prime}\right\rVert\leq n+n^{\prime}\end{subarray}}e^{-c_{2}\left(\frac{\left\lVert x^{\prime}\right\rVert^{2}}{n^{\prime}}+\frac{\left\lVert x+x^{\prime}\right\rVert^{2}}{n+n^{\prime}}\right)}

If
\left\lVert x\right\rVert\leq 2n^{\prime},
we obtain from (
2.15
)

\textstyle\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\left\lVert x^{\prime}\right\rVert\leq n^{\prime}\\
\left\lVert x+x^{\prime}\right\rVert\leq n+n^{\prime}\end{subarray}}e^{-c_{2}\left(\frac{\left\lVert x^{\prime}\right\rVert^{2}}{n^{\prime}}+\frac{\left\lVert x+x^{\prime}\right\rVert^{2}}{n+n^{\prime}}\right)}\leq c\left[(n^{\prime})^{\frac{d}{2}}e^{-c^{\prime}\frac{\left\lVert x\right\rVert^{2}}{n+n^{\prime}}}+(n+n^{\prime})^{\frac{d}{2}}e^{-c^{\prime}\frac{\left\lVert x\right\rVert^{2}}{n^{\prime}}}\right].

Substituting this result above,

\displaystyle J_{1,1}

\displaystyle\textstyle\leq c\eta(z)^{-(d-2)}+c\sum\limits_{n^{\prime}\geq\frac{\left\lVert x\right\rVert}{2}}\left[(n+n^{\prime})^{-\frac{d}{2}}e^{-c^{\prime}\frac{\left\lVert x\right\rVert^{2}}{n+n^{\prime}}}+(n^{\prime})^{-\frac{d}{2}}e^{-c^{\prime}\frac{\left\lVert x\right\rVert^{2}}{n^{\prime}}}\right]

\displaystyle\textstyle\leq c\eta(z)^{-(d-2)}+2c\sum\limits_{n^{\prime}>\frac{\left\lVert x\right\rVert}{2}}(n^{\prime})^{-\frac{d}{2}}e^{-c^{\prime}\frac{\left\lVert x\right\rVert^{2}}{n^{\prime}}}.

Split the appearing sum in the line above into
\frac{\left\lVert x\right\rVert}{2}\leq n^{\prime}\leq\left\lVert x\right\rVert^{2}
and
n^{\prime}>\left\lVert x\right\rVert^{2};
bound the first resulting sum by (
2.14
) to obtain an upper bound of the form
c\left\lVert x\right\rVert^{-(d-2)},
and the second, by
\sum\limits_{n^{\prime}>\left\lVert x\right\rVert^{2}}(n^{\prime})^{-\frac{d}{2}}e^{-c^{\prime}\frac{\left\lVert x\right\rVert^{2}}{n^{\prime}}}\leq\sum\limits_{n^{\prime}>\left\lVert x\right\rVert^{2}}(n^{\prime})^{-\frac{d}{2}}\asymp\left\lVert x\right\rVert^{-(d-2)},
the last
\asymp
was obtained from (
2.13
). We have established,

(I)

\textstyle J_{1,1}\leq c\eta(z)^{-(d-2)},

c
being a universal constant depending solely on dimension.

We now will handle
J_{1,2}.
The hypotheses give

\displaystyle J_{1,2}

\displaystyle\textstyle\leq c_{1}^{2}\sum\limits_{n^{\prime}\geq 0}\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\left\lVert x^{\prime}\right\rVert\leq n^{\prime}\\
\left\lVert x+x^{\prime}\right\rVert>n+n^{\prime}\end{subarray}}(n^{\prime})^{-\frac{d}{2}}e^{-c_{2}\left(\left\lVert z+z^{\prime}\right\rVert+\frac{\left\lVert x^{\prime}\right\rVert^{2}}{n^{\prime}}\right)}

\displaystyle\textstyle\leq c_{1}^{2}e^{-\frac{c_{2}}{\sqrt{2}}n}\sum\limits_{n^{\prime}\geq 0}(n^{\prime})^{-\frac{d}{2}}e^{-\frac{c_{2}}{\sqrt{2}}n^{\prime}}\sum\limits_{\left\lVert x^{\prime}\right\rVert\leq n^{\prime}}e^{-\frac{c_{2}}{\sqrt{2}}\left(\left\lVert x+x^{\prime}\right\rVert+\frac{\left\lVert x^{\prime}\right\rVert^{2}}{n^{\prime}}\right)}.

It is now clear that we may bound
J_{1,2}\leq ce^{-c^{\prime}n},
with a pair of constants
c,c^{\prime}>0
depending solely on dimension. This establishes the case
n^{\frac{1}{2}}\geq\left\lVert x\right\rVert
and thus, we may assume now
\left\lVert x\right\rVert>n^{\frac{1}{2}}
in what follows and bound
e^{-\frac{c_{2}}{\sqrt{2}}n}\leq 1.
Consider an integer
0\leq n^{\prime}<\left\lVert x\right\rVert,
invoking (
2.12
) we reach
\sum\limits_{\left\lVert x^{\prime}\right\rVert\leq n^{\prime}}e^{-\frac{c_{2}}{\sqrt{2}}\left(\left\lVert x+x^{\prime}\right\rVert+\frac{\left\lVert x^{\prime}\right\rVert^{2}}{n^{\prime}}\right)}\leq ce^{-c^{\prime}\left\lVert x\right\rVert},

a fortiori

c_{1}^{2}\sum\limits_{0\leq n^{\prime}<\left\lVert x\right\rVert}(n^{\prime})^{-\frac{d}{2}}\sum\limits_{\left\lVert x^{\prime}\right\rVert\leq n^{\prime}}e^{-\frac{c_{2}}{\sqrt{2}}\left(\left\lVert x+x^{\prime}\right\rVert+\frac{\left\lVert x^{\prime}\right\rVert^{2}}{n^{\prime}}\right)}\leq ce^{-c^{\prime}\left\lVert x\right\rVert},
for universal constants
c,c^{\prime}>0.
If
n^{\prime}\geq\left\lVert x\right\rVert,
bound
\left\lVert x+x^{\prime}\right\rVert\geq 0
and use (
2.11
):
\sum\limits_{\left\lVert x^{\prime}\right\rVert\leq n^{\prime}}e^{-\frac{c_{2}}{\sqrt{2}}\left(\left\lVert x+x^{\prime}\right\rVert+\frac{\left\lVert x^{\prime}\right\rVert^{2}}{n^{\prime}}\right)}\leq c(n^{\prime})^{\frac{d}{2}},
and then
\sum\limits_{n^{\prime}\geq\left\lVert x\right\rVert}(n^{\prime})^{-\frac{d}{2}}e^{-\frac{c_{2}}{\sqrt{2}}n^{\prime}}\sum\limits_{\left\lVert x^{\prime}\right\rVert\leq n^{\prime}}e^{-\frac{c_{2}}{\sqrt{2}}\left(\left\lVert x+x^{\prime}\right\rVert+\frac{\left\lVert x^{\prime}\right\rVert^{2}}{n^{\prime}}\right)}\leq c\sum\limits_{n^{\prime}\geq\left\lVert x\right\rVert}e^{-\frac{c_{2}}{\sqrt{2}}n^{\prime}}\asymp e^{-\frac{c_{2}}{\sqrt{2}}\left\lVert x\right\rVert}.
Thus, we may assert, there exists a constant
c>0
such that for all
z=(n,x)
satisfying
\left\lVert x\right\rVert>n^{\frac{1}{2}},

J_{1,2}\leq c\eta(z)^{-(d-2)};
the same type of bound for
\left\lVert x\right\rVert\leq n^{\frac{1}{2}}
was established already. Bearing in mind these bounds, (
I
), and since
J_{1}=J_{1,1}+J_{1,2},
we have finally established

(J)

\textstyle J_{1}\leq c\eta(z)^{-(d-2)},

with
c>0
is a constant that does not depend on
z.

We will now prove the upper bound for
J_{2}.
Similarly as was done with
J_{1},
we split
J_{2}=J_{2,1}+J_{2,2},
being
J_{2,1}=\sum\limits_{n^{\prime}\geq 0}\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\left\lVert x^{\prime}\right\rVert>n^{\prime}\\
\left\lVert x+x^{\prime}\right\rVert\leq n+n^{\prime}\end{subarray}}\varphi(z^{\prime})\varphi(z^{\prime}+z)
and
J_{2,2}=\sum\limits_{n^{\prime}\geq 0}\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\left\lVert x^{\prime}\right\rVert>n^{\prime}\\
\left\lVert x+x^{\prime}\right\rVert>n+n^{\prime}\end{subarray}}\varphi(z^{\prime})\varphi(z^{\prime}+z).
Here,
J_{2,2}
is easy for we have for we may repeat the calculations of (
E
) and obtain

(K)

\textstyle J_{2,2}\leq c_{1}^{2}\sum\limits_{z^{\prime}\in\mathds{Z}^{d+1}}e^{-c_{2}(\left\lVert z^{\prime}\right\rVert+\left\lVert z+z^{\prime}\right\rVert)}\leq ce^{-c^{\prime}\left\lVert z\right\rVert},

with
c_{1},c_{2}
the constants in the hypothesis and
c,c^{\prime}
two positive universal constants. There remains to handle
J_{2,1}.
Here we may bound

\displaystyle J_{2,1}

\displaystyle\textstyle\leq c_{1}^{2}\sum\limits_{n^{\prime}\geq 0}\sum\limits_{\left\lVert x+x^{\prime}\right\rVert\leq n+n^{\prime}}e^{-c_{2}\left(\left\lVert z^{\prime}\right\rVert+\frac{\left\lVert x+x^{\prime}\right\rVert^{2}}{n+n^{\prime}}\right)}(n+n^{\prime})^{-\frac{d}{2}}

\displaystyle\textstyle\leq c_{1}^{2}\sum\limits_{n^{\prime}\geq 0}(n+n^{\prime})^{-\frac{d}{2}}e^{-\frac{c_{2}}{\sqrt{2}}n^{\prime}}\sum\limits_{\left\lVert x+x^{\prime}\right\rVert\leq n+n^{\prime}}e^{-\frac{c_{2}}{\sqrt{2}}\left(\left\lVert x^{\prime}\right\rVert+\frac{\left\lVert x+x^{\prime}\right\rVert^{2}}{n+n^{\prime}}\right)}

\displaystyle\textstyle=c_{1}^{2}\sum\limits_{n^{\prime}\geq 0}(n+n^{\prime})^{-\frac{d}{2}}e^{-\frac{c_{2}}{\sqrt{2}}n^{\prime}}\sum\limits_{\left\lVert x^{\prime}\right\rVert\leq n+n^{\prime}}e^{-\frac{c_{2}}{\sqrt{2}}\left(\left\lVert x^{\prime}-x\right\rVert+\frac{\left\lVert x^{\prime}\right\rVert^{2}}{n+n^{\prime}}\right)}.

Suppose first that
\left\lVert x\right\rVert\leq n^{\frac{1}{2}}.
The bounds of (
2.12
) give
\sum\limits_{\left\lVert x^{\prime}\right\rVert\leq n+n^{\prime}}e^{-\frac{c_{2}}{\sqrt{2}}\left(\left\lVert x^{\prime}-x\right\rVert+\frac{\left\lVert x^{\prime}\right\rVert^{2}}{n+n^{\prime}}\right)}\leq ce^{-c^{\prime}\frac{\left\lVert x\right\rVert^{2}}{n+n^{\prime}}}\leq c,
for a pair of constants
c,c^{\prime}>0.
Then,
J_{2,1}\leq c\sum\limits_{n^{\prime}\geq 0}(n+n^{\prime})^{-\frac{d}{2}}\leq c^{\prime}n^{-\frac{d}{2}+1}=c^{\prime}\eta(z)^{-(d-2)},
where the constants
c,c^{\prime}
are positive and depending on nothing but, perhaps, dimension. This would finish the bound for
J_{2,1}
under the assumption
\left\lVert x\right\rVert\leq n^{\frac{1}{2}}.
Next, assume
n^{\frac{1}{2}}<\left\lVert x\right\rVert.
Here, we further split (we remind the reader the sums are over
n^{\prime}\in\mathds{Z}_{+}
and
x^{\prime}\in\mathds{Z}^{d}
as stated)

\displaystyle J_{2,1}

\displaystyle\textstyle\leq c_{1}^{2}\sum\limits_{0\leq n^{\prime}+n\leq\left\lVert x\right\rVert}(n+n^{\prime})^{-\frac{d}{2}}e^{-\frac{c_{2}}{\sqrt{2}}n^{\prime}}\sum\limits_{\left\lVert x^{\prime}\right\rVert\leq n+n^{\prime}}e^{-\frac{c_{2}}{\sqrt{2}}\left(\left\lVert x^{\prime}-x\right\rVert+\frac{\left\lVert x^{\prime}\right\rVert^{2}}{n+n^{\prime}}\right)}

\displaystyle\textstyle+c_{1}^{2}\sum\limits_{n^{\prime}+n>\left\lVert x\right\rVert}(n+n^{\prime})^{-\frac{d}{2}}e^{-\frac{c_{2}}{\sqrt{2}}n^{\prime}}\sum\limits_{\left\lVert x^{\prime}\right\rVert\leq n+n^{\prime}}e^{-\frac{c_{2}}{\sqrt{2}}\left(\left\lVert x^{\prime}-x\right\rVert+\frac{\left\lVert x^{\prime}\right\rVert^{2}}{n+n^{\prime}}\right)}.

If
\left\lVert x\right\rVert\geq n^{\prime}+n
then the inner sum is bounded by an expression of the form
ce^{-c^{\prime}\left\lVert x\right\rVert},
again by (
2.12
). This yields
c_{1}^{2}\sum\limits_{0\leq n^{\prime}+n\leq\left\lVert x\right\rVert}(n+n^{\prime})^{-\frac{d}{2}}e^{-\frac{c_{2}}{\sqrt{2}}n^{\prime}}\sum\limits_{\left\lVert x^{\prime}\right\rVert\leq n+n^{\prime}}e^{-\frac{c_{2}}{\sqrt{2}}\left(\left\lVert x^{\prime}-x\right\rVert+\frac{\left\lVert x^{\prime}\right\rVert^{2}}{n+n^{\prime}}\right)}\leq ce^{-c^{\prime}\left\lVert x\right\rVert}\leq C\eta(z)^{-(d-2)},
with
c,c^{\prime},C>0
universal constants. If
\left\lVert x\right\rVert<n+n^{\prime},
then the inner sum is bounded by an expression of the form
ce^{-c^{\prime}\frac{\left\lVert x\right\rVert^{2}}{n+n^{\prime}}},
by the same point. Thus, there exist two constants
c,c^{\prime}
such that for all
z,

\textstyle c_{1}^{2}\sum\limits_{n^{\prime}+n>\left\lVert x\right\rVert}(n+n^{\prime})^{-\frac{d}{2}}e^{-\frac{c_{2}}{\sqrt{2}}n^{\prime}}\sum\limits_{\left\lVert x^{\prime}\right\rVert\leq n+n^{\prime}}e^{-\frac{c_{2}}{\sqrt{2}}\left(\left\lVert x^{\prime}-x\right\rVert+\frac{\left\lVert x^{\prime}\right\rVert^{2}}{n+n^{\prime}}\right)}\leq c\sum\limits_{n^{\prime}+n>\left\lVert x\right\rVert}(n+n^{\prime})^{-\frac{d}{2}}e^{-c^{\prime}\left(n^{\prime}+\frac{\left\lVert x\right\rVert^{2}}{n+n^{\prime}}\right)}.

We may now use (
2.13
) and (
2.14
) as follows:

\displaystyle\textstyle\sum\limits_{n^{\prime}+n>\left\lVert x\right\rVert}(n+n^{\prime})^{-\frac{d}{2}}e^{-c^{\prime}\left(n^{\prime}+\frac{\left\lVert x\right\rVert^{2}}{n+n^{\prime}}\right)}

\displaystyle\textstyle\leq\sum\limits_{n^{\prime}+n\leq\left\lVert x\right\rVert^{2}}(n+n^{\prime})^{-\frac{d}{2}}e^{-c^{\prime}\frac{\left\lVert x\right\rVert^{2}}{n+n^{\prime}}}+\sum\limits_{n^{\prime}+n>\left\lVert x\right\rVert^{2}}(n+n^{\prime})^{-\frac{d}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq c\left\lVert x\right\rVert^{-(d-2)}+c^{\prime}\left\lVert x\right\rVert^{-(d-2)}=(c+c^{\prime})\left\lVert x\right\rVert^{-(d-2)}.

Thus, we showed that there exists a constant
c>0
such that for all
z\in\mathds{Z}^{d+1},

(L)

\textstyle J_{2,1}\leq c\eta(z)^{-(d-2)}.

Then, by (
K
) and (
L
), we reach
J_{2}\leq c\eta(z)^{-(d-2)},
with
c
not depending on
z.
Furthermore, the previous bound on
J_{2}
together with (
J
) show that there exists a
c>0
such that for all vertices
z
in
\Gamma_{d}(\lambda),

(M)

\textstyle J\leq c\eta(z)^{-(d-2)}.

Combining the results of (
E
), (
H
) and (
M
), we finally reach that
\sum\limits_{z^{\prime}\in\mathds{Z}^{d+1}}\varphi(z^{\prime}-z)\varphi(z^{\prime})\leq c\eta(z)^{-\frac{d}{2}+1}.

II. Lower bound.
Assume the hypothesis for the lower bound. Then

\textstyle\sum\limits_{z^{\prime}\in\mathds{Z}^{d+1}}\varphi(z^{\prime}-z)\psi(z^{\prime})\geq\sum\limits_{n^{\prime}>\max\left(n,\left\lVert x\right\rVert^{2}\right)}\sum\limits_{\left\lVert x^{\prime}-x\right\rVert^{2}\leq n^{\prime}-n}\varphi(z^{\prime}-z)\psi(z^{\prime}).

The two relations
\left\lVert x\right\rVert^{2}<n^{\prime}
and
\left\lVert x^{\prime}-x\right\rVert^{2}\leq n^{\prime}-n
imply
\left\lVert x^{\prime}\right\rVert^{2}\leq 2\left\lVert x\right\rVert^{2}+2\left\lVert x^{\prime}-x\right\rVert^{2}\leq 4n^{\prime}.
Then, the bounds of the hypothesis give (the last
\asymp
follows from (
2.13
))

\displaystyle\textstyle\sum\limits_{n^{\prime}>\max\left(n,\left\lVert x\right\rVert^{2}\right)}

\displaystyle\textstyle\displaystyle\sum\limits_{\left\lVert x^{\prime}-x\right\rVert^{2}\leq n^{\prime}-n}\varphi(z^{\prime}-z)\psi(z^{\prime})

\displaystyle\textstyle\displaystyle\geq c\sum\limits_{n^{\prime}>\max\left(n,\left\lVert x\right\rVert^{2}\right)}(n^{\prime})^{-\frac{d}{2}}(n^{\prime}-n)^{-\frac{d}{2}}\sum\limits_{\left\lVert x^{\prime}-x\right\rVert^{2}\leq n^{\prime}-n}e^{-c^{\prime}\frac{\left\lVert x^{\prime}\right\rVert^{2}}{n^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle\textstyle\displaystyle\geq c\sum\limits_{n^{\prime}>\max\left(n,\left\lVert x\right\rVert^{2}\right)}(n^{\prime})^{-\frac{d}{2}}(n^{\prime}-n)^{-\frac{d}{2}}\sum\limits_{\left\lVert x^{\prime}-x\right\rVert^{2}\leq n^{\prime}-n}e^{-4c^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\textstyle\displaystyle\geq c^{\prime\prime}\sum\limits_{n^{\prime}>\max\left(n,\left\lVert x\right\rVert^{2}\right)}(n^{\prime})^{-\frac{d}{2}}\asymp\max\left(n,\left\lVert x\right\rVert^{2}\right)^{-\frac{d}{2}+1}.

The lower bounds have been substantiated keeping in mind (
A
).
∎

With the two lemmas proved, we are in conditions to finish the theorem. By virtue of (
A
), (
B
), (
C
) and (
6.2.1
) we have that
\mathsf{USF}(\mathscr{C}(0,z))\asymp\mathds{E}{}\left(K_{z}\right)=\sum\limits_{z^{\prime}\in\mathds{Z}^{d+1}}G(z,z^{\prime})G(0,z^{\prime}).
Applying (
6.2.2
) (bear in mind the Green’s function bounds (
2.22
)), we reach that
\mathsf{USF}(\mathscr{C}(0,z))\asymp\eta(z)^{-(d-2)}.

∎

6.2
An upper bound on the probability that finitely many points are
\mathsf{USF}
-connected

We are going to generalise the previous result to any subset of vertices. We will follow closely
[
BKPS04
]
. First, we introduce the definition of
spread
of a set of vertices. For any vertex
z\in\mathds{Z}^{d+1}
and any finite subset
\mathrm{W}\subset\mathds{Z}^{d+1}
(with two or more vertices) we define their spreads (relative to the metric
\eta
) as

(6.3)

\textstyle\begin{split}\left\langle z\right\rangle_{\eta}&=\max(1,\eta(z))\\
\left\langle\mathrm{W}\right\rangle_{\eta}&\textstyle=\min\limits_{\mathrm{E}}\prod\limits_{\{z,z^{\prime}\}\in\mathrm{E}}\left\langle z-z^{\prime}\right\rangle_{\eta},\end{split}

where the minimum runs over
all
\mathrm{E}\subset\mathscr{P}(\mathrm{W})
making
(\mathrm{W},\mathrm{E})
an undirected spanning tree.
If
\mathrm{W}
consists of two (different) vertices
\alpha
and
\beta,
then
\left\langle\mathrm{W}\right\rangle_{\eta}=\eta(\alpha-\beta);
in this case we will write
\left\langle\alpha\beta\right\rangle_{\eta}
in lieu of
\left\langle\mathrm{W}\right\rangle_{\eta}.
Observe that (
4.5
) defines the spread of a point relative to the Euclidean metric. For simplicity, if
\mathrm{W}=\{\alpha,\beta,\ldots,\xi\},
we will write
\left\langle\alpha\beta\cdots\xi\right\rangle_{\eta},
in particular,
\left\langle 0z\right\rangle_{\eta}=\left\langle z\right\rangle_{\eta}.
Observe that
[
BKPS04
]
also defined the concept of “spread of subset of vertices of
\mathds{Z}^{d}
” but they use the Euclidean metric (see their Definition 2.1). When
\mathrm{W}=\{\alpha,\beta,\gamma\},
then

\textstyle\left\langle\mathrm{W}\right\rangle_{\eta}=\min\{\left\langle\alpha\beta\right\rangle_{\eta}\left\langle\alpha\gamma\right\rangle_{\eta},\left\langle\alpha\beta\right\rangle_{\eta}\left\langle\beta\gamma\right\rangle_{\eta},\left\langle\alpha\gamma\right\rangle_{\eta}\left\langle\beta\gamma\right\rangle_{\eta}\}.

Proposition ( 6.4
)

[
BKPS04
